   290	 succeeded in 0ms:
   291	     1	# baton — cross-harness workflow failover (v1 plan)
   292	     2	
   293	     3	Working name: **baton** (rename is trivial; final name is Saksham's call before repo creation).
   294	     4	
   295	     5	## Context
   296	     6	
   297	     7	Saksham's AI-coding workflow spans three harnesses — Claude Code, OpenAI Codex CLI, and Cursor — and breaks whenever one platform's usage limits run out mid-task. Work state, plan context, and the writer/reviewer role assignments are trapped in the dying session. His handwritten notes describe a three-layer system (ideal-loop creator with smoke-test gate; dual-worktree adversarial write/review pipeline; cross-platform workflow portability). **Milestone 1 (this plan): the cross-platform failover layer** — chosen by Saksham as the foundation, since it addresses the stated pain point and the other layers run on top of it.
   298	     8	
   299	     9	Research confirmed the white space: Databricks' Omnigent (June 2026) is the closest analog but replaces native configs with its own YAML and ships **no usage-limit failover**; nothing else does harness-level failover either (closest is model-level switching). The portability standards to build on already exist: AGENTS.md (Linux Foundation/AAIF-governed; native in Codex + Cursor; bridged into Claude Code via an `@AGENTS.md` import in CLAUDE.md) and the Agent Skills standard (SKILL.md; native in Claude Code + Codex).
   300	    10	
   301	    11	## Locked decisions (from Saksham, this session)
   302	    12	
   303	    13	1. **First milestone**: cross-platform failover layer. Loop-creator and dual-worktree pipelines are later milestones; leave seams.
   304	    14	2. **Capture**: continuous checkpointing via harness hooks **plus** an explicit `/handoff` finalize command that records reason-for-switching.
   305	    15	3. **Platforms**: all three from v1. Claude Code gets the deep integration (plugin with hooks); Codex and Cursor get lighter native adapters.
   306	    16	4. **Receive flow**: intake (original platform + reason) → load bundle → role-matrix remap → auto-continue the task.
   307	    17	5. **Bundle home**: gitignored `.handoff/` directory inside the repo.
   308	    18	6. **Architecture**: one repo — harness-agnostic core Node CLI + three native adapters + templates + tests.
   309	    19	7. **Attribution (hard invariant)**: ZERO AI mentions in git history — no `Co-Authored-By`, no "Generated with Claude Code" — permanently. During initial construction (pre-v1), all commits are authored solely by `SakshamUboweja <ssakshamu@gmail.com>` with frequent, regular commits to main (contributions accrue to his GitHub profile); external human OSS contributors are welcome post-v1. Enforced via `{"attribution": {"commit": "", "pr": ""}}` in Claude Code settings (the old `includeCoAuthoredBy` boolean is deprecated) + a doctor check.
   310	    20	8. **AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md**: the tool scaffolds an ideal pair into any project (this repo dogfoods them). Design grounded in the official Fable 5 and GPT 5.6 prompting guides (fetched; distilled below). Strongest models get latitude on the design.
   311	    21	9. **Development methodology** (governs building this repo AND is encoded in the templates as the product's workflow):
   312	    22	   - Fable 5 writes the plan doc → **GPT 5.6 Sol xhigh reviews it (Gate 1)**.
   313	    23	   - Decompose into tasks/subtasks.
   314	    24	   - Per subtask: **Opus 4.8 writes unit tests first, GPT 5.5 xhigh verifies the tests**; the two keep each other in line during implementation.
   315	    25	   - At the end: **GPT 5.6 Sol xhigh and Fable 5 xhigh run independent reviews**; feedback is collated and implemented.
   316	    26	   - Overall: a goal with genuine constraints, looped until the constraints are met. Review artifacts under `reviews/<task-id>/` with verdicts `{APPROVED, APPROVED_WITH_NOTES, BLOCKED}` (pattern from Saksham's wt-* repos).
   317	    27	
   318	    28	## Research findings that shape the design (verified 2026-07-11)
   319	    29	
   320	    30	- **Codex plugin (installed, v1.0.4) is the implementation pattern**: single Node entrypoint invoked as `node "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/<x>.mjs" <subcmd>`; hooks in `hooks/hooks.json` (SessionStart/SessionEnd/Stop) read event JSON on stdin and can emit `{"decision":"block","reason":…}`; commands/agents/skills as markdown with frontmatter; per-workspace state dir; structured-output JSON schemas for reviews. It has **no usage-limit handling** — our failover logic is net-new.
   321	    31	- **Superpowers plugin (installed) is the cross-harness packaging pattern**: one repo ships `hooks/hooks.json` (Claude) + `hooks-cursor.json` (Cursor, camelCase events), a polyglot `run-hook.cmd`, and `.codex-plugin` / `.cursor-plugin` / `AGENTS.md` descriptors. Hook output differs per harness (Claude: `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext`; Cursor: `additional_context`) — branch on `$CURSOR_PLUGIN_ROOT`.
   322	    32	- **Limit-detection signals** (Gate-1 verified, 2026-07-11): structured signals FIRST, text signatures as fallback tier.
   323	    33	  - Claude Code: **`StopFailure` hook event** fires when a turn ends on an API error, with matcher filtering by error type (`rate_limit`, `overloaded`, `billing_error`, `server_error`, …) — the primary, structured limit-death signal. Output/exit code ignored (observability-only), so a checkpoint there is safe by construction. Text fallback: the stable strings `You've hit your session limit · resets …` / `weekly limit` / `Opus limit`.
   324	    34	  - Codex (>= 0.144): **lifecycle hooks exist** (`SessionStart`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `SubagentStop`, `Stop`; configured via `~/.codex/hooks.json`, `<repo>/.codex/hooks.json`, or config.toml `[hooks]`; stdin JSON with `session_id`, `cwd`, `model`, `transcript_path`). No StopFailure equivalent → limit death bounded by last Stop checkpoint. Text fallback: `You've hit your usage limit…`; `codex exec --json` exits non-zero.
   325	    35	  - Cursor: weakest — BYO-key returns `{"error":"Too Many Requests", …}`; managed-billing schema undocumented. Heuristics + timeouts, `confidence: low`.
   326	    36	- **Session handoff prior art**: HANDOFF.md markdown is the accepted lossy-universal mechanism; schema-level session transplants (authsec-bridge) are brittle — v1 stays markdown+JSON, no session-file surgery.
   327	    37	- **Local surfaces available on this machine**: `codex` CLI (gpt-5.6-sol, xhigh) with empty-but-supported `~/.codex/prompts/`; `cursor-agent` CLI (reads AGENTS.md + CLAUDE.md natively); `.agents/skills` is the official Codex skills path; Cursor project hooks via `.cursor/hooks.json` (onPreEdit/onPostEdit/onPreCommit/onApprove).
   328	    38	- **Running the methodology's models from Claude Code**: Agent tool model overrides (opus/fable) + codex companion (`… codex-companion.mjs task --model gpt-5.6-sol|gpt-5.5 --effort xhigh`, read-only sandbox, structured output).
   329	    39	
   330	    40	## Prompting-guide distillation (for the AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md templates)
   331	    41	
   332	    42	- **Fable 5** (platform.claude.com guide): strong instruction-following — brief steering beats enumeration; state boundaries (pause only for destructive/irreversible/scope-change/user-only input); "when you have enough information to act, act"; audit progress claims against tool evidence; parallel subagents with async communication; provide a memory/notes location; fresh-context verifier subagents beat self-critique; over-prescriptive skills degrade output; never instruct reasoning echo (triggers refusals).
   333	    43	- **GPT 5.6** (developers.openai.com guide): hierarchy Role → Goal → Success criteria → Constraints → Tools → Stop rules; outcome definition over process prescription; trimming repeated rules improved evals 10–15% and cut tokens 41–66%; ALWAYS/NEVER only for true invariants; state each policy once; name the current phase for long work; explicit validation guidance; avoid contradictions, universal defaults, blanket brevity, personality words.
   334	    44	- **Template consequence**: shared invariants live exactly once in AGENTS.md (lean, hierarchical); CLAUDE.md is an `@AGENTS.md` import plus a short Claude-specific block (memory location, subagent guidance); any Codex-specific steering gets its own clearly-scoped short section.
   335	    45	
   336	    46	## Design
   337	    47	
   338	    48	_(Sections below synthesized from two Plan-agent designs — core engine; adapters/templates/methodology — merged after Gate-1 review.)_
   339	    49	
   340	    50	### Core engine (`core/`)
   341	    51	
   342	    52	**Language/tooling**: plain ESM JavaScript (`.mjs`) + JSDoc types checked by `tsc --noEmit --checkJs` (dev-only dep). No build step — hooks invoke source directly (`node "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/…"`), same as the proven codex plugin. **Zero runtime dependencies**; arg parsing via `node:util` `parseArgs`; **`node:test`** as the framework (golden-file helper ~40 lines with `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1`). Node >= 20. Every command's `run()` takes an injected `io` object (`{cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now}`); pure logic (reducers/renderers/resolvers/classifiers) is data-in/data-out; tests run over an in-memory fs fake plus a thin real-fs integration layer.
   343	    53	
   344	    54	**CLI surface** (`baton <cmd> --json`): `checkpoint`, `finalize`, `detect`, `remap`, `receive`, `init`, `doctor`, `status`, `purge-transcript`, `recover` (forced lock recovery). Frozen exit-code contract: `0` ok · `1` command failure · `2` usage error · `10` usage-limit · `11` throttle · `12` auth · `13` other-error. `--json` mode prints exactly one envelope `{ok, data, warnings, error}` on stdout. `baton wrap -- <cmd>` (PTY supervisor) is **deferred post-v1** — hook-driven detection suffices; the name/contract is reserved.
   345	    55	
   346	    56	**Bundle** (`.handoff/`): single active bundle = `bundle.json` (atomic tmp+rename snapshot, `.bak` of previous good) + `journal.ndjson` (append-only events, torn-tail tolerant) + rendered `HANDOFF.md` + `history/` (unified retention: last 10 entries **per type** — finalize freezes, takeover archives, receive archives — each = frozen snapshot plus rotated journal; receipts needed for replay/audit are always preserved within retained entries). Load = snapshot + replay journal entries past `journalSeq`; recovery paths: `.bak` + replay, or full journal rebuild. Schema `baton/bundle@1`: origin platform/model, task goal/constraints/acceptance, plan steps with status, capped decisions log, files touched, role assignments, git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, handoff finalize block (`status: open|sealed|received` + `receive_log[]`), compaction markers. Size discipline is **deterministic truncation only** (decisions capped first-20+last-150 with marker; no LLM summarization).
   347	    57	
   348	    58	**Concurrency** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): all bundle mutations run under a repo-scoped lock — `.handoff/lock` acquired via atomic `mkdir`/`O_EXCL`, containing `{writerId: <platform>-<pid>-<sessionHint>, fencingToken: <random per acquisition>, acquiredAt, heartbeatAt}`. **Lock model (iterations 3+4)**: mutation locks are **operation-scoped** — acquired for the duration of one command's mutation and released, never held across turns; logical session ownership lives in `bundle.json` (the active `origin`/generation), not in the lock. Lock metadata (host, pid, process start time, fencing token) is written atomically with acquisition; a crash between lock creation and metadata publication reads as torn metadata. Recovery rules, conservative on every OS: automatic recovery only when the owner is **provably dead** (same host, pid gone or pid/start-time mismatch); a same-host owner confirmed alive is **refused** — including `recover --force` — with the instruction to terminate that process first; missing/torn/cross-host/unverifiable owner metadata is treated as **unsupported for automatic recovery** (refusal with explanation), never as safe-to-steal. Fencing tokens remain a fast-abort layer. Tests: force attempted against a live paused writer (refused), crash between lock creation and metadata publication, owner paused after its final token check, torn-metadata refusal. Journal entries carry `writerId`; sequence numbers are allocated under the lock. **Semantic isolation**: v1 enforces one active task — a checkpoint whose `sessionHint`/origin doesn't match the active bundle is rejected with an actionable warning (`--take-over` supersedes: archives the current bundle to `history/` and starts fresh), so two unrelated sessions can't merge goals/files/decisions into one handoff. `receive` is a core-owned two-phase transaction. `receive --prepare` validates + resolves roles + returns a one-time receipt token and the resume prompt without mutating state; the token **binds** the bundle revision (journalSeq), normalized intake, config digest, target platform+session, the probe snapshot used for remap, **and a content-sensitive prepare-time git digest** — HEAD + branch + hashes of the staged and unstaged diffs + a bounded untracked-files digest (path + size + content hash, capped) — so a file that stays `M` but changes content still invalidates the token. `--commit` re-derives and compares every bound input, rejecting any drift (intervening checkpoint, config edit, HEAD move, content change, probe change) and requiring re-prepare; each declared drift input has its own rejection test. `receive --commit <token>` performs one atomic transition: `sealed → received` (normal) or `open → received` (the primary failure case: limit death before finalize; commit writes a receive-generated degraded seal recording intake origin/reason, flagged `degraded-seal`) — and in the same transition **adopts the receiving platform/session as the bundle's active owner and opens a fresh writable generation** (`generation` counter incremented, `status: open`), archiving the prior seal + receipt to `history/`. The receiving session's subsequent checkpoints are therefore first-party, and the bundle is immediately receivable again if the new platform also dies — chained A→B→C failover is contract-tested with checkpoints fired immediately after each receive. Cancellation or a competing receiver leaves state untouched; double-commit fails cleanly. Identical semantics on every adapter; six-direction contract tests cover sealed + unsealed variants. Tests: concurrent writers, provably-dead takeover, fencing-token abort incl. pause-after-final-check, foreign-session rejection/take-over, prepare-vs-checkpoint races, prepare-without-commit, double-commit, competing receivers, chained failovers.
   349	    59	
   350	    60	**Journal rotation & crash recovery** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): on every finalize, crash-safe order = write a rotation marker → write frozen snapshot to `history/` → rename `journal.ndjson` alongside it → start a fresh journal seeded with the snapshot's `journalSeq` → clear marker. On load, a present marker deterministically rolls the rotation forward (if the snapshot landed) or back (if not); the same marker pattern governs interrupted forced takeovers and interrupted purges (purge resumes from its marker until the whole tree is clean). Retention: last 10 rotations pruned with the history. Doctor warns at journal > 5 MB; perf test: 10k-event replay < 1 s. **Filesystem support boundary: v1 supports local filesystems only** — lock and rename atomicity are not guaranteed on network/shared mounts (NFS/SMB/cloud-sync folders); doctor detects and warns, README states it.
   351	    61	
   352	    62	**Root discovery & degraded modes** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): repo root = nearest ancestor containing `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`, else `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (worktree-aware: linked worktrees resolve to their own toplevel, shared state stays per-worktree), else cwd — `--root <path>` overrides; nested/monorepo launches therefore resolve to the same bundle deterministically, but **discovery never crosses the boundary of a nested independent git repository** (a `.git` between cwd and a candidate root stops the walk) unless `--root` says otherwise. Non-git directories: supported — `git` section is null, git-dependent features degrade with explicit warnings. Read-only sandboxes (EACCES/EROFS on `.handoff/`): checkpoint fails open with a warning naming the user-executed fallback (`baton checkpoint` run manually outside the sandbox; `receive --print-prompt` never needs writes until `--commit`). All git invocations carry a 5 s timeout (configurable); timeout → git section marked `unavailable`, never a hang.
   353	    63	
   354	    64	**Transcript policy** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): transcript-tail capture at PreCompact is **opt-in and off by default** (`capture.transcriptTail` in `baton.config.json`), bounded (last 10 messages, 8 KB cap), passed through a secret-redaction filter (common credential/token patterns; fixtures with planted secrets prove redaction), stored in a dedicated bundle field excluded from HANDOFF.md by default. `baton purge-transcript` removes the field from **every retained copy** — active snapshot, `.bak`, journal (rewrite-and-rotate under lock), and all `history/` freezes — with a test asserting planted secrets are absent from the entire `.handoff/` tree afterward. **Diagnostics logging contract (iteration 4)**: `.handoff/log/` entries are metadata-only and redacted — raw hook payloads never enter them (fixture-proven) — with bounded retention (last 50 entries / 7 days) and inclusion in the whole-tree purge test.
   355	    65	
   356	    66	**Checkpoint engine**: adapters send pre-normalized `baton/event@1` on stdin (the real API); raw hook payloads get best-effort per-platform extraction (`normalize.mjs`) that never throws — unknowns degrade to `note` events. Idempotent via dedupe keys; journal append always, snapshot rewrite throttled (≥10 events / 30 s / important event types). **Hook-safety rule: exit 0 on all soft failures** so a checkpoint can never break the host harness (`--strict` for CI). `finalize --reason "…" [--reason-class] [--to]` records the switch reason (class inferred from text via the classifier when omitted), refreshes git state, freezes history.
   357	    67	
   358	    68	**Limit detector**: two tiers — **structured signals first** (Claude Code `StopFailure` matcher error types; documented limit-specific fields in codex JSON output), text signatures as fallback. **Exit-code rule (iteration 3): a generic nonzero exit never classifies as usage-limit on its own** — exit 10 requires limit-specific structured or text evidence; nonzero without evidence = `other-error`. Fixture corpus includes non-limit failing processes. Signatures are **versioned data** (`core/data/signatures.v1.json`), seeded with the verified strings for all three platforms, matcher kinds `substring`/`regex`/`json-field`, per-signature confidence, precedence usage-limit > auth > throttle > other-error, `--signatures <path>` overlay for hot-patching drift, `--explain` for auditability. Hardening (Gate-1 additions): input is ANSI-stripped before matching; the fixture corpus includes negative/near-miss cases (throttle-vs-usage confusables, multiline, malformed JSON, localized text) so false positives are regression-tested; overlay regexes are lint-checked (length cap, no backreferences, linear-time constructs) and matching carries a time guard; a low-confidence match **never silently avoids a platform** — it warns and defers to user intake at receive. Cursor entries carry `confidence: low|medium` explicitly.
   359	    69	
   360	    70	**Role matrix**: committed `baton.config.json` at repo root (`baton/config@1`) mapping roles → ordered platform+model chains (the seven methodology roles, defined below), plus per-platform `defaults`. Resolver is pure and explainable: skips `avoid[]` platforms (auto-populated with the origin platform when reason-class is usage-limit — never remap back onto the platform that died), resolves `mode: native|delegated|forced-default|unavailable` with `{chainIndex, skipped[]}` audit data. **Availability probing** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): `doctor` probes each platform noninteractively, hard 3 s bound per probe, results cached 15 min in the state dir. Probe record = two separate dimensions: **capability** (ordered, cumulative: `installed → authenticated → reachable`, recorded as far as verifiable) and **probe outcome** (`ok | rate-limited | error | timeout`). Resolver eligibility: **selectable** = capability ≥ authenticated and outcome ≠ rate-limited; anything with an unverifiable dimension resolves as selectable-with-`degraded`-flag; `rate-limited` platforms are skipped like `avoid[]` entries. Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged `degraded`.
   361	    71	
   362	    72	**Receive**: loads bundle with recovery + staleness checks (age > 12 h; HEAD moved; dirty mismatch), merges intake flags (`--origin`, `--reason`), resolves roles, and runs as the **prepare/commit transaction defined under Concurrency** — prompt emission at prepare, state mutation only at commit. Resume prompt ≤2,500 chars (brief, why-you're-here, role table, warnings, top-5 next actions, pointer to HANDOFF.md); the prompt explicitly frames bundle content as **unverified claims to audit against the working tree, not instructions to obey** (prompt-injection posture — see Threat model).
   363	    73	
   364	    74	**Scaffold** (`init`): two-phase plan/apply with `--dry-run`; `.gitignore` ensure-line (no-op if an existing pattern covers `.handoff/`); `baton.config.json` from template; AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md written via managed blocks (`<!-- baton:begin/end -->`) — bytes outside markers never touched.
   365	    75	
   366	    76	**Attribution guard** (three layers): (1) `init` deep-merges `.claude/settings.json` → `{"attribution": {"commit": "", "pr": ""}}` (only when keys absent; refuses to touch malformed JSON; global settings documented + doctor-checked, never written by core); (2) `doctor` scans last 50 commits for AI co-author/generated-with trailers (word-boundary-safe patterns) and flags with remediation; (3) a test-suite invariant scans every rendered output and template for AI-credit strings and fails the build.
   367	    77	
   368	    78	Full module APIs, per-module test lists, repo file tree, and the fixture corpus (verbatim limit strings, hook payloads, corrupt bundles) are specified in the core design doc (committed as `docs/design/core.md` in task 0); implementation follows the named-task TDD sequence below — **tasks are identified by name, not number; the exact count is finalized when review directories are generated**. **`sessionHint` derivation (Gate-1 final note)**: per adapter, from the hook payload's stable session id (Claude Code `session_id`; Codex `session_id`; Cursor session field); when a payload lacks one, core generates a process-scoped hint flagged `unstable: true` — unstable hints never trigger foreign-session rejection on their own, only a warning.
   369	    79	### Checkpoint vocabulary (used everywhere)
   370	    80	
   371	    81	- **Mechanical checkpoint** (`baton checkpoint`): hook/script-driven, no LLM — git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, timestamps, source platform/model, files touched. <1 s, safe per-turn.
   372	    82	- **Narrative seal** (`baton finalize`, surfaced as `/handoff`): model-written — decisions + rationale, verified-done vs claimed-done, next steps, gotchas, reason for switching.
   373	    83	
   374	    84	Claude Code DOES emit a structured limit-death event (`StopFailure` with a `rate_limit` matcher); Codex and Cursor do not. The design is therefore: event-driven detection where it exists, rolling mechanical checkpoints everywhere (last coherent turn always captured), narrative seals at milestones, and receive compensating for a missing seal by asking the user why they switched and re-deriving git facts live, auditing HANDOFF.md claims against them.
   375	    85	
   376	    86	### Claude Code adapter (Tier A — highest fidelity)
   377	    87	
   378	    88	Plugin root = **repo root**: `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (author: SakshamUboweja) with path overrides pointing at `adapters/claude-code/{commands,skills,agents,hooks}` so `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` reaches `core/bin/baton.mjs` directly; `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` makes the repo its own single-entry marketplace (same shape as `openai/codex-plugin-cc`). **Spike task 0 verifies path-override support**; fallback = CI copy step vendoring `core/` under the adapter.
   379	    89	
   380	    90	Hooks (all fail-open — errors log to `.handoff/log/`, exit 0; a checkpoint must never break a session):
   381	    91	
   382	    92	| Event | Use | Timeout |
   383	    93	|---|---|---|
   384	    94	| `StopFailure` (matcher enum — e.g. `rate_limit`, `billing_error`, `server_error` — **snapshot verbatim from the official hooks reference during task 0**; fixtures generated from that canonical list; unknown future values normalize to `unknown` safely) | **The limit-death signal.** Classifies the error (matcher + payload through `baton detect`), writes a fail-open mechanical checkpoint stamped `trigger: stop-failure/<type>`, and on `rate_limit` marks the bundle limit-hit so the next SessionStart (any platform) surfaces it. Output/exit code are ignored by the harness — safe by construction. Tests cover the full snapshot enum. | 30 s |
   385	    95	| `Stop` | Routine mechanical checkpoint per assistant turn (proven codex stop-gate pattern; never emits `decision: block`) | 30 s |
   386	    96	| `PreCompact` | Snapshot context boundary before compaction; transcript-tail capture is **opt-in, off by default** (see core §transcript policy) | 60 s |
   387	    97	| `SessionEnd` | Clean-exit checkpoint | 30 s |
   388	    98	| `SessionStart` | Not a checkpoint: if a sealed or limit-hit bundle from another platform exists, inject "handoff pending — suggest `/baton:receive`" context | 10 s |
   389	    99	
   390	   100	`PostToolUse` rejected: per-tool-call spawns for incoherent mid-turn state; Stop gives the same worst-case staleness (one turn) at a fraction of the cost.
   391	   101	
   392	   102	Commands: **`/baton:handoff`** (model-invocable so Claude can propose it near limits; writes the narrative — auditing every "done" claim against tool evidence — then seals via finalize, then prints the destination's resume command); **`/baton:receive`** (`disable-model-invocation: true`; runs receive → one AskUserQuestion with two questions: origin platform with detected option first, reason for switch → ack runs the core commit transaction (received seal archived, this session adopted as owner, fresh `open` generation; double-receive prevented by the one-time token; command tests assert the active/open + archived/received outcome) → evidence audit of bundle git snapshot vs live HEAD/status → announce role-remap table → auto-continue next step, pausing only per AGENTS.md boundaries); **`/baton:baton-setup`** (doctor + choose surfaces + init + template scaffold with diff preview). Receive-side continuation is a **skill** (`handoff-protocol`), not an agent — the main thread must inherit the task; a subagent would strand context. Optional v2 `handoff-verifier` agent (fresh-context bundle audit) is out of v1 scope.
   393	   103	
   394	   104	### Codex adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven as of Codex >= 0.144)
   395	   105	
   396	   106	Gate-1 correction: Codex now ships lifecycle hooks, so the adapter is redesigned around them. Surfaces: (1) **repo `.codex/hooks.json`** (written by `init --codex`, merged never clobbered): `Stop` → mechanical checkpoint, `PreCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint, `SessionStart` → pending-handoff notice via injected context. No StopFailure equivalent exists on Codex, so a limit death is bounded by the last Stop checkpoint (one turn worst case). (2) `.agents/skills/baton-handoff/SKILL.md` per the Agent Skills standard — carries the receive/handoff protocol, invoked as `$baton-handoff` or by description match. (3) Repo `AGENTS.md` Handoff Protocol section as the always-on instruction layer (narrative checkpoints after subtasks / before risky ops / at wind-down). (4) `~/.codex/prompts/*.md` demoted to **legacy fallback only** (deprecated upstream), written only with `--with-legacy-prompts`. The notify shim is **cut** — hooks make it obsolete; `~/.codex/config.toml` is never touched. **Trust gate** (verified, Gate-1 iteration 2): Codex requires per-definition review of non-managed command hooks — trust is recorded against the hook's hash, and new/changed hooks are skipped until re-trusted via `/hooks`. Setup and doctor therefore report four distinct states per hook — installed / enabled / **trusted** / observed-executing (canary event) — and the fidelity claim is gated on the canary, with setup guiding the user through `/hooks` when review is pending. Hook definitions include `commandWindows` overrides. Fidelity statement ships verbatim in README + doctor: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via Stop hook **once trusted**; narrative staleness bounded by protocol compliance; git state always re-derived at receive. Requires codex-cli >= 0.144 (doctor enforces; older versions fall back to protocol-only Tier C with an explicit warning).
   397	   107	
   398	   108	### Cursor adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven; event names verified against official docs, Gate-1 iteration 2)
   399	   109	
   400	   110	Surfaces: `.cursor/hooks.json` (project-level, `{"version":1,"hooks":{...}}`) with the **verified** events — `stop` → mechanical checkpoint per agent turn (same semantics as Claude Code `Stop`); `afterFileEdit` → debounced checkpoint (debounce enforced by the CLI, one real checkpoint per 2 min); `beforeShellExecution` with a git-commit matcher → checkpoint before agent-issued commits (**documented limitation: commits made through the SCM UI are invisible to hooks — no pre-commit event exists**); `sessionStart` → pending-handoff notice; `preCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint. Plus `.cursor/commands/{receive,handoff,baton-setup}.md` (CLI loading unconfirmed — convenience layer; every behavior also reachable via AGENTS.md, which cursor-agent reads natively, and via the universal fallback **`baton receive --print-prompt`**) and a ≤5-line `.cursor/rules/baton-handoff.mdc` (`alwaysApply: true`). hooks.json written only after explicit confirmation, merged not clobbered; project hooks require a **trusted workspace** — doctor checks trust and gates the fidelity claim on an observed hook canary. Fidelity: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via `stop` when trusted; untrusted/cloud contexts degrade to protocol-only with an explicit warning.
   401	   111	
   402	   112	### Templates: ideal AGENTS.md + CLAUDE.md
   403	   113	
   404	   114	Design rule resolving the two-model-family tension: **every shared invariant appears exactly once in AGENTS.md**; model-specific steering is quarantined (short Claude block in CLAUDE.md; scoped Codex notes only if ever needed). Budget: AGENTS.md ≤220 lines (handoff section ≤25), enforced by a template test. ALWAYS/NEVER reserved for true invariants (attribution, destructive ops, TDD ordering); judgment calls get decision rules.
   405	   115	
   406	   116	AGENTS.md sections (template `templates/AGENTS.md.tpl`, `{{TEST_CMD}}`-style variables filled by `baton init` from detection): 1 Project (one paragraph + directory map) · 2 Roles and models (points at committed config; "resolve via `baton remap`, never hardcode model names") · 3 Working phases (research → plan → test-authoring → implementation → review → handoff, one-line entry/exit criteria, "state your phase") · 4 TDD contract (no implementation before failing tests exist and are verifier-approved; test changes re-enter verification; progress claims must cite command output) · 5 Review gates (Gate 1 plan, per-subtask test review, Gate 2 dual independent final review; verdicts APPROVED/APPROVED_WITH_NOTES/BLOCKED) · 6 Handoff protocol (when/how to checkpoint and resume) · 7 Boundaries and stop rules (never force-push/hard-reset/delete without explicit instruction; pause for scope change/irreversible/credentials; otherwise "when you have enough information to act, act") · 8 Validation (command table; done = green + gate verdict) · 9 Git and attribution (sole author SakshamUboweja; zero AI attribution invariant; **commit cadence rule: commit after every completed subtask/green TDD cycle — small, regular commits to main so contributions accrue to Saksham's GitHub profile**; never commit `.handoff/`) · 10 Memory (`.handoff/notes.md`, gitignored, rides the bundle across handoffs).
   407	   117	
   408	   118	CLAUDE.md shim (~15 lines): line 1 `@AGENTS.md`; then ≤8 Claude-specific bullets — subagent delegation for parallel research/verification, concrete per-role mechanics (reviewer roles via codex companion, test-author via Agent-tool model override), plans in `docs/plans/`, attribution-setting reminder, **commit-cadence reminder (commit each completed subtask; never batch a day's work into one commit)**, prefer `/baton:*` commands, **user-facing outputs simple and concise: outcome first, plain language, no jargon walls**. Nothing duplicating AGENTS.md; no reasoning-echo instructions.
   409	   119	
   410	   120	**One-time machine setup (first implementation act, needs your approval since it touches global files)**: append to `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` — (a) "present answers and outputs simply and concisely: outcome first, short sentences, no unnecessary detail"; (b) "when working in repos, commit completed work regularly and authored solely as SakshamUboweja"; set `{"attribution": {"commit": "", "pr": ""}}` in `~/.claude/settings.json`; save these as persistent memory.
   411	   121	
   412	   122	The baton repo dogfoods both from commit one, with a CI check (`baton init --check`) asserting rendered files match template output.
   413	   123	
   414	   124	### Encoded development methodology (single committed config: `baton.config.json`)
   415	   125	
   416	   126	Reconciled role set (the seven methodology roles supersede the earlier five-role sketch; resolver semantics from the core design apply unchanged):
   417	   127	
   418	   128	```json
   419	   129	{ "schema": "baton/config@1",
   420	   130	  "roles": {
   421	   131	    "planner":          ["claude-code/claude-fable-5"],
   422	   132	    "plan-reviewer":    ["codex/gpt-5.6-sol@xhigh", "claude-code/claude-fable-5@xhigh"],
   423	   133	    "test-author":      ["claude-code/claude-opus-4-8", "codex/gpt-5.5@xhigh"],
   424	   134	    "test-verifier":    ["codex/gpt-5.5@xhigh", "claude-code/claude-opus-4-8"],
   425	   135	    "implementer":      ["claude-code/claude-fable-5", "codex/gpt-5.6-sol@xhigh", "cursor/composer"],
   426	   136	    "final-reviewer-a": ["codex/gpt-5.6-sol@xhigh", "codex/gpt-5.5@xhigh"],
   427	   137	    "final-reviewer-b": ["claude-code/claude-fable-5@xhigh", "claude-code/claude-opus-4-8"]
   428	   138	  },
   429	   139	  "constraints": {
   430	   140	    "test-author-vs-verifier": "different-vendor-preferred, fresh-context-required",
   431	   141	    "final-review": "two-independent-fresh-context-reviews, cross-vendor-preferred"
   432	   142	  },
   433	   143	  "platforms": { "claude-code": {}, "codex": {}, "cursor": {} },
   434	   144	  "defaults": { "claude-code": "claude-fable-5", "codex": "gpt-5.6-sol", "cursor": "composer" } }
   435	   145	```
   436	   146	
   437	   147	When only one vendor is reachable, test-author/verifier fall back to different models in fresh sessions and every review artifact header carries `degraded: single-vendor` — weakening is auditable, never silent. Review artifacts extend the wt-* convention exactly: `reviews/<task-id>/{gate-1-plan, subtask-NN-tests, gate-2-final/{reviewer-a, reviewer-b, findings.md}}/iteration-NN/{prompt.md, verdict.md}`, templates ported from `wt-supervisor/reviews/_templates/`. The loop: plan doc in `docs/plans/` with goal + genuine constraints (falsifiable acceptance criteria) → Gate 1 → per-subtask test-first with the implementer forbidden from weakening/deleting tests (modifications re-enter verification) → dual independent fresh-context final reviews → collate `findings.md` → fix blocking items, re-review by the raising reviewer → loop until acceptance constraints are met with evidence attached. **Iteration budget (Saksham's rule): every review gate loops at most 5 times — hard stop after the 5th iteration; on exhaustion, the gate ends with the outstanding findings escalated to Saksham for a human decision instead of a 6th run.** This cap is encoded in the AGENTS.md template's review-gates section and applies to building this repo too.
   438	   148	
   439	   149	### Install / distribution
   440	   150	
   441	   151	| Harness | Path |
   442	   152	|---|---|
   443	   153	| Claude Code | `/plugin marketplace add SakshamUboweja/<repo>` → install (repo-as-marketplace) |
   444	   154	| Codex | `npx <pkg> init --codex` (+ root `.codex-plugin` descriptor for forward-compat) |
   445	   155	| Cursor | `npx <pkg> init --cursor` (+ `.cursor-plugin` descriptor) |
   446	   156	| Anything else | `baton receive --print-prompt` / `baton checkpoint` (pure CLI) |
   447	   157	
   448	   158	npm name availability unverified (fallback scope `@sakshamuboweja/baton`, bin `baton`). README positioning vs Omnigent: baton **complements** native configs (AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md/.cursor rules stay canonical), never wraps or proxies a harness, owns exactly one capability nobody ships — limit-failover session continuity; delete `.handoff/` and every harness works as before.
   449	   159	
   450	   160	### Build order (TDD; one commit per subtask; sole-author commits to main)
   451	   161	
   452	   162	0. **Task 0 — repo bootstrap + spikes**: create the repo with hand-rendered initial AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md/baton.config.json and the `reviews/` templates (established **once** here; the templates commit later only adds the generator + `init --check` drift test); record this plan's Gate-1 verdict(s) under `reviews/2026-07-11-baton-v1-plan/`; commit the full core design doc (module APIs + per-module test lists from the Plan-agent design) as `docs/design/core.md` so the build order is reviewable against it; spike plugin.json path-override support; verify npm name; decide vendoring fallback.
   453	   163	1. **Core tasks** (named, dependency-ordered; one commit each): repo scaffold + CI → `util/fsx` (atomic writes) → `util/jsonl` + ids → **lock/lease module** → bundle schema → merge reducer → compact → store (recovery + rotation) → HANDOFF.md render + goldens → CLI shell + status → **git snapshot** (moved before checkpoint — checkpoints need git state) → normalize → checkpoint cmd → finalize cmd (incl. journal rotation) → signatures table → classifier (incl. negative/ANSI corpus) → detect cmd → roles matrix → resolve → remap cmd → receive prompt → receive prepare/commit cmd → gitignore + managed-block scaffold → attribution merge → init cmd → git guard + doctor → attribution invariant suite → **purge-transcript cmd** (whole-tree, interruption-recovery, packaged-bin tests) → **concurrency test suite** (dual writers, provably-dead takeover, pause-after-final-fence-check, prepare-vs-checkpoint races, competing receivers) → e2e failover test (init → 25 checkpoints → kill-mid-write sim → finalize → receive → golden prompt, plus chained A→B→C failover) → contract docs. Each commit: tests first (test-author role), verified (test-verifier role), then implementation.
   454	   164	2. **Claude Code adapter** (~5 commits): hooks.json + checkpoint/session scripts (fixture-driven tests) → commands → handoff-protocol skill → plugin manifests → install smoke test.
   455	   165	3. **Codex adapter** (~3 commits): `.codex/hooks.json` writer/merger + skills templates → init --codex writer → legacy-prompts fallback (`--with-legacy-prompts`).
   456	   166	4. **Cursor adapter** (~3 commits): commands + rule + hooks.json merger → init --cursor writer → --print-prompt fallback.
   457	   167	5. **Windows + packaging** (~2 commits): Windows hook-command variants (Claude Code polyglot `.cmd` wrapper per the superpowers pattern; Codex `commandWindows`; Cursor cross-platform command strings), rename-retry on EPERM, path quoting, platform integration tests → explicit packaging commit: `files`/exports/bin/shebang in package.json, `npm pack` + fresh-install test resolving adapter paths from the packed layout, execution smoke on Linux/macOS/Windows CI.
   458	   168	6. **Gate 2**: dual independent final reviews (Sol xhigh + Fable xhigh), findings collated and fixed; loop until the acceptance constraints below pass.
   459	   169	
   460	   170	Ordering note (Gate-1 iteration 2): the **templates + methodology commits land before the core `scaffold`/`init` commits** — `init` consumes the templates, so the sequence within phase 1 is adjusted accordingly (template files + length-budget test + reviews port slot in right before the gitignore/managed-block scaffold commit); the dogfood `init --check` CI lands with `init` itself.
   461	   171	
   462	   172	### Acceptance constraints (the loop's exit condition)
   463	   173	
   464	   174	1. E2E failover test passes on real fs: a session's bundle survives a kill-mid-write, finalizes with a usage-limit reason, and produces a correct remapped resume prompt — **contract-tested in all six directions** (3 platforms × 2 directions).
   465	   175	2. `baton detect` classifies every verbatim fixture string (all three platforms) correctly with documented exit codes, and rejects the negative/near-miss corpus without false positives.
   466	   176	3. Fresh-machine simulation: `init` scaffolds all surfaces idempotently (double-run = all no-ops); `doctor` reports accurate per-harness status; `npm pack` + fresh-install + bin/shebang smoke tests pass in CI on Linux, macOS, and Windows.
   467	   177	4. Live smoke test on this machine: real handoff Claude Code → Codex and Codex → Claude Code mid-task, **plus one live Cursor direction with its hook trust/canary state observed** (a recorded protocol-only degradation is acceptable if Cursor is unavailable at test time); resume prompts inspected by Saksham.
   468	   178	5. Zero-attribution invariant: test suite scans all rendered outputs/templates; `git log` of the repo shows no AI-attributed commits and, during initial construction, exactly one author (external OSS contributors are welcome post-v1; the no-AI-attribution rule is permanent and independent).
   469	   179	6. Concurrency suite green: dual writers, provably-dead takeover with live/unknown-owner refusal, competing receivers, prepare-without-commit, chained failover.
   470	   180	7. Both final reviewers return APPROVED or APPROVED_WITH_NOTES with notes folded in.
   471	   181	
   472	   182	### Verification
   473	   183	
   474	   184	- Unit + command tests via `node --test` at every commit (red → green enforced by the methodology).
   475	   185	- The e2e failover integration test is the primary product proof; runs in CI on Node 20/22.
   476	   186	- Live-run gate (mirrors Saksham's "smoke-test feedback gate" idea from the notes): before Gate 2, run the real cross-harness handoff on this machine and present the transcript for approval.
   477	   187	- `baton doctor --strict` green on this machine (attribution settings present globally — one-time `~/.claude/settings.json` edit done during setup, with Saksham's consent since it's a global file).
   478	   188	
   479	   189	### Compatibility policy (Gate-1 addition)
   480	   190	
   481	   191	Tested version floors, enforced/reported by `doctor`: Claude Code ≥ the version documenting `StopFailure` (verify exact floor during task 0); codex-cli ≥ 0.144 (hooks; older → protocol-only Tier C with warning); cursor-agent ≥ 2026.05. Untested-newer versions get a doctor info line, not a failure. Signature table carries its own `updated` date; hooks payload drift is absorbed by the `baton/event@1` normalization seam.
   482	   192	
   483	   193	### Threat model (Gate-1 addition)
   484	   194	
   485	   195	Bundle, config, and overlay files are **untrusted inputs**: path fields from bundles are never honored as absolute paths or `..` traversals (normalized + repo-root-jailed); `.handoff/` entries that are symlinks are refused; overlay regexes are lint-gated (length, no backreferences, linear-time) with a match time guard; resume prompts frame bundle content as claims-to-audit, not instructions (mitigates a poisoned HANDOFF.md steering the receiving model); transcript capture is opt-in with secret redaction and purge. Dedicated test fixtures for each: traversal paths, symlinked bundle dir, pathological regex, planted secrets, adversarial HANDOFF.md.
   486	   196	
   487	   197	### Top risks (merged, deduplicated)
   488	   198	
   489	   199	1. **Signature drift** — versioned data table + overlay flag + `--explain` + verbatim-fixture regression corpus; unknown errors still classify as `other-error` so manual failover never blocks.
   490	   200	2. **plugin.json path-override assumption** — spike task 0; vendoring fallback ready.
   491	   201	3. **No usage-limit event on any harness / model-compliance dependence on Codex & Cursor** — rolling mechanical checkpoints bound staleness to one turn; receive-time git re-derivation + intake questions compensate; per-tier fidelity documented honestly.
   492	   202	4. **Partial-write corruption at the exact moment limits hit** — tmp+rename snapshots, `.bak`, journal replay; kill-mid-write is a first-class e2e test.
   493	   203	5. **Role-matrix rot / silent single-vendor degradation** — remap validates against live availability; `degraded:` flags in review artifacts; doctor surfaces it.
   494	   204	6. **Cursor surface uncertainty** (commands loading unconfirmed, hooks need trusted workspace) — `--print-prompt` universal fallback; empirical verification before fidelity claims.
   495	   205	7. **Template bloat degrading model output** — hard line budgets enforced by tests.
   496	   206	
   497	   207	## Out of scope for v1 (explicit seams left)
   498	   208	
   499	   209	- Ideal-loop creator with smoke-test gate (milestone 2) — hooks into `roles.resolve` delegated mode + the plan-doc format.
   500	   210	- Dual-worktree adversarial pipeline (milestone 3) — `store.mjs` already takes an explicit root; journal `source` field supports multiple writers.
   501	   211	- `baton wrap` PTY supervisor; multi-bundle `.handoff/bundles/<id>/` (single-bundle-with-locking is v1); team-shared bundles (committed mode); LLM summarization in compaction.
   502	   212

   543	codex
   544	The new plan is concise, but several of its claims depend on precise Layer-1 receive/remap semantics and on how commands are wired today. I’m narrowing to those seams now so the review can separate current facts from implementation-risk hypotheses.
   545	exec
   546	/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md | sed -n '50,120p'" in /Users/saksham/baton
   547	 succeeded in 0ms:
   548	    50	### Core engine (`core/`)
   549	    51	
   550	    52	**Language/tooling**: plain ESM JavaScript (`.mjs`) + JSDoc types checked by `tsc --noEmit --checkJs` (dev-only dep). No build step — hooks invoke source directly (`node "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/…"`), same as the proven codex plugin. **Zero runtime dependencies**; arg parsing via `node:util` `parseArgs`; **`node:test`** as the framework (golden-file helper ~40 lines with `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1`). Node >= 20. Every command's `run()` takes an injected `io` object (`{cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now}`); pure logic (reducers/renderers/resolvers/classifiers) is data-in/data-out; tests run over an in-memory fs fake plus a thin real-fs integration layer.
   551	    53	
   552	    54	**CLI surface** (`baton <cmd> --json`): `checkpoint`, `finalize`, `detect`, `remap`, `receive`, `init`, `doctor`, `status`, `purge-transcript`, `recover` (forced lock recovery). Frozen exit-code contract: `0` ok · `1` command failure · `2` usage error · `10` usage-limit · `11` throttle · `12` auth · `13` other-error. `--json` mode prints exactly one envelope `{ok, data, warnings, error}` on stdout. `baton wrap -- <cmd>` (PTY supervisor) is **deferred post-v1** — hook-driven detection suffices; the name/contract is reserved.
   553	    55	
   554	    56	**Bundle** (`.handoff/`): single active bundle = `bundle.json` (atomic tmp+rename snapshot, `.bak` of previous good) + `journal.ndjson` (append-only events, torn-tail tolerant) + rendered `HANDOFF.md` + `history/` (unified retention: last 10 entries **per type** — finalize freezes, takeover archives, receive archives — each = frozen snapshot plus rotated journal; receipts needed for replay/audit are always preserved within retained entries). Load = snapshot + replay journal entries past `journalSeq`; recovery paths: `.bak` + replay, or full journal rebuild. Schema `baton/bundle@1`: origin platform/model, task goal/constraints/acceptance, plan steps with status, capped decisions log, files touched, role assignments, git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, handoff finalize block (`status: open|sealed|received` + `receive_log[]`), compaction markers. Size discipline is **deterministic truncation only** (decisions capped first-20+last-150 with marker; no LLM summarization).
   555	    57	
   556	    58	**Concurrency** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): all bundle mutations run under a repo-scoped lock — `.handoff/lock` acquired via atomic `mkdir`/`O_EXCL`, containing `{writerId: <platform>-<pid>-<sessionHint>, fencingToken: <random per acquisition>, acquiredAt, heartbeatAt}`. **Lock model (iterations 3+4)**: mutation locks are **operation-scoped** — acquired for the duration of one command's mutation and released, never held across turns; logical session ownership lives in `bundle.json` (the active `origin`/generation), not in the lock. Lock metadata (host, pid, process start time, fencing token) is written atomically with acquisition; a crash between lock creation and metadata publication reads as torn metadata. Recovery rules, conservative on every OS: automatic recovery only when the owner is **provably dead** (same host, pid gone or pid/start-time mismatch); a same-host owner confirmed alive is **refused** — including `recover --force` — with the instruction to terminate that process first; missing/torn/cross-host/unverifiable owner metadata is treated as **unsupported for automatic recovery** (refusal with explanation), never as safe-to-steal. Fencing tokens remain a fast-abort layer. Tests: force attempted against a live paused writer (refused), crash between lock creation and metadata publication, owner paused after its final token check, torn-metadata refusal. Journal entries carry `writerId`; sequence numbers are allocated under the lock. **Semantic isolation**: v1 enforces one active task — a checkpoint whose `sessionHint`/origin doesn't match the active bundle is rejected with an actionable warning (`--take-over` supersedes: archives the current bundle to `history/` and starts fresh), so two unrelated sessions can't merge goals/files/decisions into one handoff. `receive` is a core-owned two-phase transaction. `receive --prepare` validates + resolves roles + returns a one-time receipt token and the resume prompt without mutating state; the token **binds** the bundle revision (journalSeq), normalized intake, config digest, target platform+session, the probe snapshot used for remap, **and a content-sensitive prepare-time git digest** — HEAD + branch + hashes of the staged and unstaged diffs + a bounded untracked-files digest (path + size + content hash, capped) — so a file that stays `M` but changes content still invalidates the token. `--commit` re-derives and compares every bound input, rejecting any drift (intervening checkpoint, config edit, HEAD move, content change, probe change) and requiring re-prepare; each declared drift input has its own rejection test. `receive --commit <token>` performs one atomic transition: `sealed → received` (normal) or `open → received` (the primary failure case: limit death before finalize; commit writes a receive-generated degraded seal recording intake origin/reason, flagged `degraded-seal`) — and in the same transition **adopts the receiving platform/session as the bundle's active owner and opens a fresh writable generation** (`generation` counter incremented, `status: open`), archiving the prior seal + receipt to `history/`. The receiving session's subsequent checkpoints are therefore first-party, and the bundle is immediately receivable again if the new platform also dies — chained A→B→C failover is contract-tested with checkpoints fired immediately after each receive. Cancellation or a competing receiver leaves state untouched; double-commit fails cleanly. Identical semantics on every adapter; six-direction contract tests cover sealed + unsealed variants. Tests: concurrent writers, provably-dead takeover, fencing-token abort incl. pause-after-final-check, foreign-session rejection/take-over, prepare-vs-checkpoint races, prepare-without-commit, double-commit, competing receivers, chained failovers.
   557	    59	
   558	    60	**Journal rotation & crash recovery** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): on every finalize, crash-safe order = write a rotation marker → write frozen snapshot to `history/` → rename `journal.ndjson` alongside it → start a fresh journal seeded with the snapshot's `journalSeq` → clear marker. On load, a present marker deterministically rolls the rotation forward (if the snapshot landed) or back (if not); the same marker pattern governs interrupted forced takeovers and interrupted purges (purge resumes from its marker until the whole tree is clean). Retention: last 10 rotations pruned with the history. Doctor warns at journal > 5 MB; perf test: 10k-event replay < 1 s. **Filesystem support boundary: v1 supports local filesystems only** — lock and rename atomicity are not guaranteed on network/shared mounts (NFS/SMB/cloud-sync folders); doctor detects and warns, README states it.
   559	    61	
   560	    62	**Root discovery & degraded modes** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): repo root = nearest ancestor containing `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`, else `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (worktree-aware: linked worktrees resolve to their own toplevel, shared state stays per-worktree), else cwd — `--root <path>` overrides; nested/monorepo launches therefore resolve to the same bundle deterministically, but **discovery never crosses the boundary of a nested independent git repository** (a `.git` between cwd and a candidate root stops the walk) unless `--root` says otherwise. Non-git directories: supported — `git` section is null, git-dependent features degrade with explicit warnings. Read-only sandboxes (EACCES/EROFS on `.handoff/`): checkpoint fails open with a warning naming the user-executed fallback (`baton checkpoint` run manually outside the sandbox; `receive --print-prompt` never needs writes until `--commit`). All git invocations carry a 5 s timeout (configurable); timeout → git section marked `unavailable`, never a hang.
   561	    63	
   562	    64	**Transcript policy** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): transcript-tail capture at PreCompact is **opt-in and off by default** (`capture.transcriptTail` in `baton.config.json`), bounded (last 10 messages, 8 KB cap), passed through a secret-redaction filter (common credential/token patterns; fixtures with planted secrets prove redaction), stored in a dedicated bundle field excluded from HANDOFF.md by default. `baton purge-transcript` removes the field from **every retained copy** — active snapshot, `.bak`, journal (rewrite-and-rotate under lock), and all `history/` freezes — with a test asserting planted secrets are absent from the entire `.handoff/` tree afterward. **Diagnostics logging contract (iteration 4)**: `.handoff/log/` entries are metadata-only and redacted — raw hook payloads never enter them (fixture-proven) — with bounded retention (last 50 entries / 7 days) and inclusion in the whole-tree purge test.
   563	    65	
   564	    66	**Checkpoint engine**: adapters send pre-normalized `baton/event@1` on stdin (the real API); raw hook payloads get best-effort per-platform extraction (`normalize.mjs`) that never throws — unknowns degrade to `note` events. Idempotent via dedupe keys; journal append always, snapshot rewrite throttled (≥10 events / 30 s / important event types). **Hook-safety rule: exit 0 on all soft failures** so a checkpoint can never break the host harness (`--strict` for CI). `finalize --reason "…" [--reason-class] [--to]` records the switch reason (class inferred from text via the classifier when omitted), refreshes git state, freezes history.
   565	    67	
   566	    68	**Limit detector**: two tiers — **structured signals first** (Claude Code `StopFailure` matcher error types; documented limit-specific fields in codex JSON output), text signatures as fallback. **Exit-code rule (iteration 3): a generic nonzero exit never classifies as usage-limit on its own** — exit 10 requires limit-specific structured or text evidence; nonzero without evidence = `other-error`. Fixture corpus includes non-limit failing processes. Signatures are **versioned data** (`core/data/signatures.v1.json`), seeded with the verified strings for all three platforms, matcher kinds `substring`/`regex`/`json-field`, per-signature confidence, precedence usage-limit > auth > throttle > other-error, `--signatures <path>` overlay for hot-patching drift, `--explain` for auditability. Hardening (Gate-1 additions): input is ANSI-stripped before matching; the fixture corpus includes negative/near-miss cases (throttle-vs-usage confusables, multiline, malformed JSON, localized text) so false positives are regression-tested; overlay regexes are lint-checked (length cap, no backreferences, linear-time constructs) and matching carries a time guard; a low-confidence match **never silently avoids a platform** — it warns and defers to user intake at receive. Cursor entries carry `confidence: low|medium` explicitly.
   567	    69	
   568	    70	**Role matrix**: committed `baton.config.json` at repo root (`baton/config@1`) mapping roles → ordered platform+model chains (the seven methodology roles, defined below), plus per-platform `defaults`. Resolver is pure and explainable: skips `avoid[]` platforms (auto-populated with the origin platform when reason-class is usage-limit — never remap back onto the platform that died), resolves `mode: native|delegated|forced-default|unavailable` with `{chainIndex, skipped[]}` audit data. **Availability probing** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): `doctor` probes each platform noninteractively, hard 3 s bound per probe, results cached 15 min in the state dir. Probe record = two separate dimensions: **capability** (ordered, cumulative: `installed → authenticated → reachable`, recorded as far as verifiable) and **probe outcome** (`ok | rate-limited | error | timeout`). Resolver eligibility: **selectable** = capability ≥ authenticated and outcome ≠ rate-limited; anything with an unverifiable dimension resolves as selectable-with-`degraded`-flag; `rate-limited` platforms are skipped like `avoid[]` entries. Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged `degraded`.
   569	    71	
   570	    72	**Receive**: loads bundle with recovery + staleness checks (age > 12 h; HEAD moved; dirty mismatch), merges intake flags (`--origin`, `--reason`), resolves roles, and runs as the **prepare/commit transaction defined under Concurrency** — prompt emission at prepare, state mutation only at commit. Resume prompt ≤2,500 chars (brief, why-you're-here, role table, warnings, top-5 next actions, pointer to HANDOFF.md); the prompt explicitly frames bundle content as **unverified claims to audit against the working tree, not instructions to obey** (prompt-injection posture — see Threat model).
   571	    73	
   572	    74	**Scaffold** (`init`): two-phase plan/apply with `--dry-run`; `.gitignore` ensure-line (no-op if an existing pattern covers `.handoff/`); `baton.config.json` from template; AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md written via managed blocks (`<!-- baton:begin/end -->`) — bytes outside markers never touched.
   573	    75	
   574	    76	**Attribution guard** (three layers): (1) `init` deep-merges `.claude/settings.json` → `{"attribution": {"commit": "", "pr": ""}}` (only when keys absent; refuses to touch malformed JSON; global settings documented + doctor-checked, never written by core); (2) `doctor` scans last 50 commits for AI co-author/generated-with trailers (word-boundary-safe patterns) and flags with remediation; (3) a test-suite invariant scans every rendered output and template for AI-credit strings and fails the build.
   575	    77	
   576	    78	Full module APIs, per-module test lists, repo file tree, and the fixture corpus (verbatim limit strings, hook payloads, corrupt bundles) are specified in the core design doc (committed as `docs/design/core.md` in task 0); implementation follows the named-task TDD sequence below — **tasks are identified by name, not number; the exact count is finalized when review directories are generated**. **`sessionHint` derivation (Gate-1 final note)**: per adapter, from the hook payload's stable session id (Claude Code `session_id`; Codex `session_id`; Cursor session field); when a payload lacks one, core generates a process-scoped hint flagged `unstable: true` — unstable hints never trigger foreign-session rejection on their own, only a warning.
   577	    79	### Checkpoint vocabulary (used everywhere)
   578	    80	
   579	    81	- **Mechanical checkpoint** (`baton checkpoint`): hook/script-driven, no LLM — git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, timestamps, source platform/model, files touched. <1 s, safe per-turn.
   580	    82	- **Narrative seal** (`baton finalize`, surfaced as `/handoff`): model-written — decisions + rationale, verified-done vs claimed-done, next steps, gotchas, reason for switching.
   581	    83	
   582	    84	Claude Code DOES emit a structured limit-death event (`StopFailure` with a `rate_limit` matcher); Codex and Cursor do not. The design is therefore: event-driven detection where it exists, rolling mechanical checkpoints everywhere (last coherent turn always captured), narrative seals at milestones, and receive compensating for a missing seal by asking the user why they switched and re-deriving git facts live, auditing HANDOFF.md claims against them.
   583	    85	
   584	    86	### Claude Code adapter (Tier A — highest fidelity)
   585	    87	
   586	    88	Plugin root = **repo root**: `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (author: SakshamUboweja) with path overrides pointing at `adapters/claude-code/{commands,skills,agents,hooks}` so `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` reaches `core/bin/baton.mjs` directly; `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` makes the repo its own single-entry marketplace (same shape as `openai/codex-plugin-cc`). **Spike task 0 verifies path-override support**; fallback = CI copy step vendoring `core/` under the adapter.
   587	    89	
   588	    90	Hooks (all fail-open — errors log to `.handoff/log/`, exit 0; a checkpoint must never break a session):
   589	    91	
   590	    92	| Event | Use | Timeout |
   591	    93	|---|---|---|
   592	    94	| `StopFailure` (matcher enum — e.g. `rate_limit`, `billing_error`, `server_error` — **snapshot verbatim from the official hooks reference during task 0**; fixtures generated from that canonical list; unknown future values normalize to `unknown` safely) | **The limit-death signal.** Classifies the error (matcher + payload through `baton detect`), writes a fail-open mechanical checkpoint stamped `trigger: stop-failure/<type>`, and on `rate_limit` marks the bundle limit-hit so the next SessionStart (any platform) surfaces it. Output/exit code are ignored by the harness — safe by construction. Tests cover the full snapshot enum. | 30 s |
   593	    95	| `Stop` | Routine mechanical checkpoint per assistant turn (proven codex stop-gate pattern; never emits `decision: block`) | 30 s |
   594	    96	| `PreCompact` | Snapshot context boundary before compaction; transcript-tail capture is **opt-in, off by default** (see core §transcript policy) | 60 s |
   595	    97	| `SessionEnd` | Clean-exit checkpoint | 30 s |
   596	    98	| `SessionStart` | Not a checkpoint: if a sealed or limit-hit bundle from another platform exists, inject "handoff pending — suggest `/baton:receive`" context | 10 s |
   597	    99	
   598	   100	`PostToolUse` rejected: per-tool-call spawns for incoherent mid-turn state; Stop gives the same worst-case staleness (one turn) at a fraction of the cost.
   599	   101	
   600	   102	Commands: **`/baton:handoff`** (model-invocable so Claude can propose it near limits; writes the narrative — auditing every "done" claim against tool evidence — then seals via finalize, then prints the destination's resume command); **`/baton:receive`** (`disable-model-invocation: true`; runs receive → one AskUserQuestion with two questions: origin platform with detected option first, reason for switch → ack runs the core commit transaction (received seal archived, this session adopted as owner, fresh `open` generation; double-receive prevented by the one-time token; command tests assert the active/open + archived/received outcome) → evidence audit of bundle git snapshot vs live HEAD/status → announce role-remap table → auto-continue next step, pausing only per AGENTS.md boundaries); **`/baton:baton-setup`** (doctor + choose surfaces + init + template scaffold with diff preview). Receive-side continuation is a **skill** (`handoff-protocol`), not an agent — the main thread must inherit the task; a subagent would strand context. Optional v2 `handoff-verifier` agent (fresh-context bundle audit) is out of v1 scope.
   601	   103	
   602	   104	### Codex adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven as of Codex >= 0.144)
   603	   105	
   604	   106	Gate-1 correction: Codex now ships lifecycle hooks, so the adapter is redesigned around them. Surfaces: (1) **repo `.codex/hooks.json`** (written by `init --codex`, merged never clobbered): `Stop` → mechanical checkpoint, `PreCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint, `SessionStart` → pending-handoff notice via injected context. No StopFailure equivalent exists on Codex, so a limit death is bounded by the last Stop checkpoint (one turn worst case). (2) `.agents/skills/baton-handoff/SKILL.md` per the Agent Skills standard — carries the receive/handoff protocol, invoked as `$baton-handoff` or by description match. (3) Repo `AGENTS.md` Handoff Protocol section as the always-on instruction layer (narrative checkpoints after subtasks / before risky ops / at wind-down). (4) `~/.codex/prompts/*.md` demoted to **legacy fallback only** (deprecated upstream), written only with `--with-legacy-prompts`. The notify shim is **cut** — hooks make it obsolete; `~/.codex/config.toml` is never touched. **Trust gate** (verified, Gate-1 iteration 2): Codex requires per-definition review of non-managed command hooks — trust is recorded against the hook's hash, and new/changed hooks are skipped until re-trusted via `/hooks`. Setup and doctor therefore report four distinct states per hook — installed / enabled / **trusted** / observed-executing (canary event) — and the fidelity claim is gated on the canary, with setup guiding the user through `/hooks` when review is pending. Hook definitions include `commandWindows` overrides. Fidelity statement ships verbatim in README + doctor: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via Stop hook **once trusted**; narrative staleness bounded by protocol compliance; git state always re-derived at receive. Requires codex-cli >= 0.144 (doctor enforces; older versions fall back to protocol-only Tier C with an explicit warning).
   605	   107	
   606	   108	### Cursor adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven; event names verified against official docs, Gate-1 iteration 2)
   607	   109	
   608	   110	Surfaces: `.cursor/hooks.json` (project-level, `{"version":1,"hooks":{...}}`) with the **verified** events — `stop` → mechanical checkpoint per agent turn (same semantics as Claude Code `Stop`); `afterFileEdit` → debounced checkpoint (debounce enforced by the CLI, one real checkpoint per 2 min); `beforeShellExecution` with a git-commit matcher → checkpoint before agent-issued commits (**documented limitation: commits made through the SCM UI are invisible to hooks — no pre-commit event exists**); `sessionStart` → pending-handoff notice; `preCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint. Plus `.cursor/commands/{receive,handoff,baton-setup}.md` (CLI loading unconfirmed — convenience layer; every behavior also reachable via AGENTS.md, which cursor-agent reads natively, and via the universal fallback **`baton receive --print-prompt`**) and a ≤5-line `.cursor/rules/baton-handoff.mdc` (`alwaysApply: true`). hooks.json written only after explicit confirmation, merged not clobbered; project hooks require a **trusted workspace** — doctor checks trust and gates the fidelity claim on an observed hook canary. Fidelity: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via `stop` when trusted; untrusted/cloud contexts degrade to protocol-only with an explicit warning.
   609	   111	
   610	   112	### Templates: ideal AGENTS.md + CLAUDE.md
   611	   113	
   612	   114	Design rule resolving the two-model-family tension: **every shared invariant appears exactly once in AGENTS.md**; model-specific steering is quarantined (short Claude block in CLAUDE.md; scoped Codex notes only if ever needed). Budget: AGENTS.md ≤220 lines (handoff section ≤25), enforced by a template test. ALWAYS/NEVER reserved for true invariants (attribution, destructive ops, TDD ordering); judgment calls get decision rules.

   720	   146	
   721	   147	When only one vendor is reachable, test-author/verifier fall back to different models in fresh sessions and every review artifact header carries `degraded: single-vendor` — weakening is auditable, never silent. Review artifacts extend the wt-* convention exactly: `reviews/<task-id>/{gate-1-plan, subtask-NN-tests, gate-2-final/{reviewer-a, reviewer-b, findings.md}}/iteration-NN/{prompt.md, verdict.md}`, templates ported from `wt-supervisor/reviews/_templates/`. The loop: plan doc in `docs/plans/` with goal + genuine constraints (falsifiable acceptance criteria) → Gate 1 → per-subtask test-first with the implementer forbidden from weakening/deleting tests (modifications re-enter verification) → dual independent fresh-context final reviews → collate `findings.md` → fix blocking items, re-review by the raising reviewer → loop until acceptance constraints are met with evidence attached. **Iteration budget (Saksham's rule): every review gate loops at most 5 times — hard stop after the 5th iteration; on exhaustion, the gate ends with the outstanding findings escalated to Saksham for a human decision instead of a 6th run.** This cap is encoded in the AGENTS.md template's review-gates section and applies to building this repo too.

   749	   175	2. `baton detect` classifies every verbatim fixture string (all three platforms) correctly with documented exit codes, and rejects the negative/near-miss corpus without false positives.
   750	   176	3. Fresh-machine simulation: `init` scaffolds all surfaces idempotently (double-run = all no-ops); `doctor` reports accurate per-harness status; `npm pack` + fresh-install + bin/shebang smoke tests pass in CI on Linux, macOS, and Windows.
   751	   177	4. Live smoke test on this machine: real handoff Claude Code → Codex and Codex → Claude Code mid-task, **plus one live Cursor direction with its hook trust/canary state observed** (a recorded protocol-only degradation is acceptable if Cursor is unavailable at test time); resume prompts inspected by Saksham.
   752	   178	5. Zero-attribution invariant: test suite scans all rendered outputs/templates; `git log` of the repo shows no AI-attributed commits and, during initial construction, exactly one author (external OSS contributors are welcome post-v1; the no-AI-attribution rule is permanent and independent).
   753	   179	6. Concurrency suite green: dual writers, provably-dead takeover with live/unknown-owner refusal, competing receivers, prepare-without-commit, chained failover.

   786	    16	// The ten user-facing commands are the frozen v1 contract; session-start is an
   787	    17	// adapter-facing addition prescribed by gate-2 finding 8 (the cheap
   788	    18	// SessionStart gate Codex/Cursor hooks call — always exit 0).
   789	    19	const COMMANDS = [
   790	    20	  'checkpoint',
   791	    21	  'finalize',
   792	    22	  'detect',
   793	    23	  'remap',
   794	    24	  'receive',
   795	    25	  'init',

   802	    32	
   803	    33	function version() {
   804	    34	  const pkgPath = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'package.json');
   805	    35	  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(pkgPath, 'utf8')).version;
   806	    36	}
   807	    37	
   808	    38	const USAGE = `usage: baton <command> [options]
   809	    39	
   810	    40	commands:
   811	    41	  ${COMMANDS.join(', ')}
   812	    42	
   813	    43	global options:
   814	    44	  --version   print version
   815	    45	  --json      machine-readable output: one compact JSON envelope on stdout
   816	    46	`;
   817	    47	
   818	    48	/**
   819	    49	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
   820	    50	 * @param {any} io
   821	    51	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
   822	    52	 */
   823	    53	function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
   824	    54	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
   825	    55	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
   826	    56	}
   827	    57	
   828	    58	/**
   829	    59	 * @param {string[]} argv process args after the binary
   830	    60	 * @param {any} io injected {cwd, stdout, stderr}
   831	    61	 * @returns {number | Promise<number>} exit code
   832	    62	 */
   833	    63	export function run(argv, io) {
   834	    64	  const args = argv.filter((a) => a !== '');
   835	    65	  // Global --json intent is parsed BEFORE dispatch (gate-2 iter-2 M6): a
   836	    66	  // top-level `--json` with no command, or --help/--version, must still emit
   837	    67	  // exactly one envelope rather than raw text.
   838	    68	  const jsonWanted = args.includes('--json');
   839	    69	  const cmdIndex = args.findIndex((a) => !a.startsWith('--'));
   840	    70	  const cmd = cmdIndex === -1 ? undefined : args[cmdIndex];
   841	    71	
   842	    72	  if (cmd === undefined) {
   843	    73	    // No command — just flags (or nothing).
   844	    74	    if (args.includes('--version')) {
   845	    75	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { version: version() } });
   846	    76	      else io.stdout.write(version() + '\n');
   847	    77	      return 0;
   848	    78	    }
   849	    79	    if (args.includes('--help')) {
   850	    80	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage: USAGE.trim() } });
   851	    81	      else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
   852	    82	      return 0;
   853	    83	    }
   854	    84	    // Bare invocation (or a lone --json): a usage error.
   855	    85	    if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'no command given' } });
   856	    86	    else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
   857	    87	    return 2;
   858	    88	  }
   859	    89	
   860	    90	  // All flags (before and after the command token) reach the command; only the
   861	    91	  // command token itself is stripped, so a leading `--json` isn't lost.
   862	    92	  const rest = args.filter((_, i) => i !== cmdIndex);
   863	    93	
   864	    94	  // Usage errors honor the --json envelope contract too (gate-2 fix 11 + M6:
   865	    95	  // global --json counts, wherever it sits).
   866	    96	  const wantsJson = jsonWanted;
   867	    97	  if (cmd === 'wrap') {
   868	    98	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1' } });
   869	    99	    else io.stderr.write(`baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1.\n${USAGE}`);
   870	   100	    return 2;
   871	   101	  }
   872	   102	  if (!COMMANDS.includes(cmd)) {
   873	   103	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: `unknown command '${cmd}'` } });
   874	   104	    else io.stderr.write(`baton: unknown command '${cmd}'\n${USAGE}`);
   875	   105	    return 2;
   876	   106	  }
   877	   107	
   878	   108	  if (rest.includes('--help')) {
   879	   109	    // Subcommand help honors the global --json envelope too (iter-3 F8): with
   880	   110	    // --json it must emit exactly one envelope, never raw usage text.
   881	   111	    const usage = `usage: baton ${cmd} [options]\n\nSee README for the ${cmd} contract.`;
   882	   112	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage } });
   883	   113	    else io.stdout.write(`${usage}\n`);
   884	   114	    return 0;
   885	   115	  }
   886	   116	
   887	   117	  if (cmd === 'status') return cmdStatus(rest, io);
   888	   118	  if (cmd === 'detect') return cmdDetect(rest, io);
   889	   119	  if (cmd === 'remap') return cmdRemap(rest, io);
   890	   120	  if (cmd === 'checkpoint') return cmdCheckpoint(rest, io);
   891	   121	  if (cmd === 'finalize') return cmdFinalize(rest, io);
   892	   122	  if (cmd === 'receive') return cmdReceive(rest, io);
   893	   123	  if (cmd === 'init') return cmdInit(rest, io);
   894	   124	  if (cmd === 'doctor') return cmdDoctor(rest, io);
   895	   125	  if (cmd === 'purge-transcript') return cmdPurgeTranscript(rest, io);
   896	   126	  if (cmd === 'session-start') return cmdSessionStart(rest, io);
   897	   127	  if (cmd === 'recover') return cmdRecover(rest, io);
   898	   128	
   899	   129	  const error = { code: 'not-implemented', msg: `baton ${cmd}: not implemented yet` };
   900	   130	  if (rest.includes('--json')) {
   901	   131	    emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error });
   902	   132	  } else {
   903	   133	    io.stderr.write(`${error.msg}\n`);
   904	   134	  }
   905	   135	  return 1;
   906	   136	}
