  7273	Verified facts:
  7274	The current CLI has only the v1 command set; `loop` and `pipeline` are new surfaces, while `wrap` is still reserved as future PTY supervisor work ([core/src/cli.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/cli.mjs:16), [core/src/cli.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/cli.mjs:97)). The existing role matrix has seven roles and no `merger` or `subtask-review` role ([baton.config.json](/Users/saksham/baton/baton.config.json:3)). Existing receive is a two-phase transaction whose token binds generation, journal seq, handoff status, intake, config digest, git digest, probe digest, target platform, and target session ([core/src/receive/txn.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/receive/txn.mjs:54), [core/src/receive/txn.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/receive/txn.mjs:80)); stale commits are rejected ([core/src/receive/txn.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/receive/txn.mjs:242)). Existing checkpointing enforces one active stable session per bundle and rejects foreign stable sessions unless `--take-over` is used ([core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs:265)). Linked worktree commands will resolve their own worktree root because root discovery stops at nearest `.handoff`, `baton.config.json`, or `.git` ([core/src/commands/shared.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/shared.mjs:27)). Current `.gitignore` ignores `.handoff/` but not `.worktrees/` ([.gitignore](/Users/saksham/baton/.gitignore:1)).

  7276	Hypotheses:
  7277	The headless child contract will fail in practice unless the plan controls process groups, hook side effects, prompt echo in verdict parsing, live CLI permission modes, and smoke approval staleness. These are not implementation nits; they are the core reliability surface of this milestone.

  7279	VERDICT: BLOCKED
  7280	FINDINGS:
  7281	1. [blocking] — The plan violates the role-matrix invariant and introduces unresolved roles/hardcoded model identities. It says roles resolve through `baton.config.json` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:41)), but the phase list uses `subtask-review` and the pipeline uses a `merger role (Fable)` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:38), [docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:85)). Those roles are absent from the committed matrix ([baton.config.json](/Users/saksham/baton/baton.config.json:3)), and hardcoding “Sol / Claude / Fable” conflicts with the repo rule that concrete models live in config, not docs/prompts/code ([AGENTS.md](/Users/saksham/baton/AGENTS.md:16)). — Fix: Define exact role IDs for writer, reviewer, subtask reviewer, and merger in `baton.config.json`/template, or map them explicitly to existing roles. Remove concrete model/vendor names from the plan and child prompts; make loop spec validation fail when a referenced role is missing.
  7282	
  7283	2. [blocking] — The central loop state does not compose with Layer-1 root discovery and one-active-session semantics. The plan wants one root `.handoff/loop` state plus two persistent worktrees ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:48), [docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:78)), but existing root discovery in a linked worktree will resolve the worktree root, not the main root ([core/src/commands/shared.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/shared.mjs:27)). Existing checkpoint rejects stable foreign sessions in the same bundle ([core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs:275)), so simultaneous writer/reviewer child hooks can either fork state into per-worktree `.handoff/` dirs or be rejected/corrupt takeover flow. — Fix: Specify a single authoritative root passed to every child via `--root`, or explicitly namespace per-worker bundles. Define whether child hooks are disabled, redirected, or supervisor-owned. Add tests with two stable child session hints from two worktrees writing/checkpointing concurrently.
  7284	
  7285	3. [blocking] — The failover chain is underspecified relative to the receive transaction. The plan says `detect → finalize → remap → receive → relaunch` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:66)), but `baton remap` is read-only ([core/src/commands/remap.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/remap.mjs:6)), and `receive` requires prepare/commit with identical bound inputs and session/root/probe/git state ([core/src/commands/receive.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/receive.mjs:33), [core/src/commands/receive.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/receive.mjs:64)). Probe cache and git state are token inputs ([core/src/receive/txn.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/receive/txn.mjs:65)), so any supervisor write, probe refresh, ff-sync, or smoke artifact change between prepare and commit can invalidate the token. — Fix: Replace the chain with an exact transaction: freeze child output, classify, finalize or degraded-open receive, refresh probes before prepare, prepare+commit with identical flags/session/root and no intervening writes, then relaunch. Add stale-token and retry tests around loop-state writes, git changes, probe-cache changes, and open-bundle limit death.
  7286	
  7287	4. [blocking] — “Role’s next chain entry” is not supported for model-level failures, including the degradation case in this review. The resolver skips by platform avoid/rate-limit/disabled/auth state, not by `{platform, model}` ([core/src/roles/resolve.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/roles/resolve.mjs:16)). It selects the first eligible entry in each configured role chain ([core/src/roles/resolve.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/roles/resolve.mjs:61)). If `codex/gpt-5.6-sol` is account-tier rejected but Codex itself is usable, the loop can retry the same rejected model forever or avoid all Codex entries and skip valid `codex/gpt-5.5` fallbacks. — Fix: Add model-entry failure state or an `avoidEntries[]` mechanism, classify model-unavailable separately from usage-limit, and test `gpt-5.6-sol rejected → codex/gpt-5.5 selected` without platform-wide avoidance.
  7288	
  7289	5. [major] — The worktree discipline lacks corruption guards. The plan says two persistent worktrees are gitignored and branch-jailed, with only the merger writing main ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:78)), but current `.gitignore` does not ignore `.worktrees/` ([.gitignore](/Users/saksham/baton/.gitignore:1)). It also does not define stale worktree adoption, branch-name namespace, dirty-worktree refusal, merge-conflict parking, concurrent main movement, or `git worktree remove/prune` cleanup. HYPOTHESIS: raw deletion of `.worktrees/` can leave Git’s linked-worktree metadata stale. — Fix: Define `worktrees.mjs` as a strict Git transaction layer: namespaced branches, clean-index preflight, expected branch/HEAD assertions before and after each child, main merge lock, ff-only sync checks, conflict parking, and prune/remove cleanup. Add adversarial tests for stale branches, dirty workers, main moved, merge conflict, and untracked `.worktrees/`.
  7290	
  7291	6. [major] — The attribution invariant is not actually covered for child worktree commits. The plan claims the existing doctor guard covers main and both worktree branches ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:89)), but current doctor scans only the last 50 commits reachable from the current repo invocation ([core/src/commands/doctor.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/doctor.mjs:229)). The hard repo invariant requires sole author and zero AI attribution everywhere ([AGENTS.md](/Users/saksham/baton/AGENTS.md:55)). — Fix: Make the supervisor set `GIT_AUTHOR_*` and `GIT_COMMITTER_*` for every child, disable Claude attribution settings where applicable, and gate every branch merge on `git log` scans over the exact worker branch and main range for author/email plus forbidden trailers. Acceptance must include child commits from both worktrees.
  7292	
  7293	7. [major] — The supervisor spawn and verdict-tail contract is too weak for a v1 automation surface. The plan relies on plain final-tail parsing ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:52)), but does not specify process-group lifecycle, timeout kill/reap behavior, output truncation, prompt-echo defense, hook/log noise separation, or what happens when a child exits after edits but before a verdict. The existing detector classifies generic nonzero as `other-error`, not usage-limit ([core/src/detect/classifier.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/detect/classifier.mjs:81)). — Fix: Define child supervision as a contract: detached supervisor PID/lock, process groups, SIGTERM/SIGKILL escalation, transcript files with byte caps, parser over the child’s final assistant message only or structured output schema, and tests for prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated transcript, timeout, zombie grandchild, and non-limit failure.
  7294	
  7295	8. [major] — The smoke gate can approve stale state. The plan parks after smoke and resumes with `--approve-smoke` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:61)), but it does not bind approval to smoke output, git state, loop state, or the exact command. Existing git snapshots include a content-sensitive digest that could be used for this ([core/src/git/snapshot.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/git/snapshot.mjs:17)). — Fix: Write a smoke approval record with digest of loop state, command, output, HEAD, dirty/contentDigest, and child assignment. Require `--approve-smoke <token>` and revalidate before continuing; otherwise park for a fresh smoke run.

  7318	4. [blocking] — “Role’s next chain entry” is not supported for model-level failures, including the degradation case in this review. The resolver skips by platform avoid/rate-limit/disabled/auth state, not by `{platform, model}` ([core/src/roles/resolve.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/roles/resolve.mjs:16)). It selects the first eligible entry in each configured role chain ([core/src/roles/resolve.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/roles/resolve.mjs:61)). If `codex/gpt-5.6-sol` is account-tier rejected but Codex itself is usable, the loop can retry the same rejected model forever or avoid all Codex entries and skip valid `codex/gpt-5.5` fallbacks. — Fix: Add model-entry failure state or an `avoidEntries[]` mechanism, classify model-unavailable separately from usage-limit, and test `gpt-5.6-sol rejected → codex/gpt-5.5 selected` without platform-wide avoidance.
  7319	
  7320	5. [major] — The worktree discipline lacks corruption guards. The plan says two persistent worktrees are gitignored and branch-jailed, with only the merger writing main ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:78)), but current `.gitignore` does not ignore `.worktrees/` ([.gitignore](/Users/saksham/baton/.gitignore:1)). It also does not define stale worktree adoption, branch-name namespace, dirty-worktree refusal, merge-conflict parking, concurrent main movement, or `git worktree remove/prune` cleanup. HYPOTHESIS: raw deletion of `.worktrees/` can leave Git’s linked-worktree metadata stale. — Fix: Define `worktrees.mjs` as a strict Git transaction layer: namespaced branches, clean-index preflight, expected branch/HEAD assertions before and after each child, main merge lock, ff-only sync checks, conflict parking, and prune/remove cleanup. Add adversarial tests for stale branches, dirty workers, main moved, merge conflict, and untracked `.worktrees/`.
  7321	
  7322	6. [major] — The attribution invariant is not actually covered for child worktree commits. The plan claims the existing doctor guard covers main and both worktree branches ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:89)), but current doctor scans only the last 50 commits reachable from the current repo invocation ([core/src/commands/doctor.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/doctor.mjs:229)). The hard repo invariant requires sole author and zero AI attribution everywhere ([AGENTS.md](/Users/saksham/baton/AGENTS.md:55)). — Fix: Make the supervisor set `GIT_AUTHOR_*` and `GIT_COMMITTER_*` for every child, disable Claude attribution settings where applicable, and gate every branch merge on `git log` scans over the exact worker branch and main range for author/email plus forbidden trailers. Acceptance must include child commits from both worktrees.
  7323	
  7324	7. [major] — The supervisor spawn and verdict-tail contract is too weak for a v1 automation surface. The plan relies on plain final-tail parsing ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:52)), but does not specify process-group lifecycle, timeout kill/reap behavior, output truncation, prompt-echo defense, hook/log noise separation, or what happens when a child exits after edits but before a verdict. The existing detector classifies generic nonzero as `other-error`, not usage-limit ([core/src/detect/classifier.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/detect/classifier.mjs:81)). — Fix: Define child supervision as a contract: detached supervisor PID/lock, process groups, SIGTERM/SIGKILL escalation, transcript files with byte caps, parser over the child’s final assistant message only or structured output schema, and tests for prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated transcript, timeout, zombie grandchild, and non-limit failure.
  7325	
  7326	8. [major] — The smoke gate can approve stale state. The plan parks after smoke and resumes with `--approve-smoke` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:61)), but it does not bind approval to smoke output, git state, loop state, or the exact command. Existing git snapshots include a content-sensitive digest that could be used for this ([core/src/git/snapshot.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/git/snapshot.mjs:17)). — Fix: Write a smoke approval record with digest of loop state, command, output, HEAD, dirty/contentDigest, and child assignment. Require `--approve-smoke <token>` and revalidate before continuing; otherwise park for a fresh smoke run.

    12	const BUILTIN_SIGNATURES = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'data', 'signatures.v1.json');
    13	const STALE_AFTER_MS = 12 * 60 * 60 * 1000;

    15	/** @typedef {{platform: string, origin: string, reason: string, gitSnapshot: any, probes?: any, sessionHint: string, sessionUnstable?: boolean, reasonClass?: string}} ReceiveOpts */
    16	
    17	/**
    18	 * Classify the switch reason, KEEPING confidence (gate-2 iter-2 M4): a
    19	 * low-confidence heuristic (e.g. Cursor's "quota exceeded") must not silently
    20	 * drive an avoid[] decision — the caller warns and defers to explicit intake.
    21	 * @param {string} reason @param {string} originPlatform @param {any} io
    22	 * @returns {{class: string | null, confidence: string | null}}
    23	 */
    24	function classifyReason(reason, originPlatform, io) {
    25	  try {
    26	    const table = loadSignatures({ builtinPath: BUILTIN_SIGNATURES }, io);
    27	    const verdict = classify({ text: reason, exitCode: 0, platform: originPlatform, table, structured: null });
    28	    return verdict.class === 'ok' ? { class: null, confidence: null } : { class: verdict.class, confidence: verdict.confidence };
    29	  } catch {
    30	    return { class: null, confidence: null };
    31	  }
    32	}
    33	
    34	/**
    35	 * Effective intake: the sealed bundle already records who handed off and why,
    36	 * so the 'unknown'/'unspecified' CLI sentinels default from it (audit finding:
    37	 * the documented resume command rendered "Handed off from unknown" straight
    38	 * after sealing a real origin/reason). Explicit intake always wins. Pure and
    39	 * bundle-deterministic, so prepare and commit derive identical tokens.
    40	 * @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @returns {ReceiveOpts}
    41	 */
    42	function effectiveIntake(bundle, opts) {
    43	  const originSentinel = opts.origin === 'unknown' || opts.origin === undefined;
    44	  const reasonSentinel = opts.reason === 'unspecified' || opts.reason === undefined;
    45	  const sealedOrigin = typeof bundle?.origin?.platform === 'string' && bundle.origin.platform !== 'unknown' ? bundle.origin.platform : null;
    46	  const sealedReason = typeof bundle?.handoff?.reason === 'string' && bundle.handoff.reason.length > 0 ? bundle.handoff.reason : null;
    47	  return {
    48	    ...opts,
    49	    origin: originSentinel && sealedOrigin ? sealedOrigin : opts.origin,
    50	    reason: reasonSentinel && sealedReason ? sealedReason : opts.reason,
    51	  };
    52	}
    53	
    54	// The bound token inputs, in the fixed order the structured token serializes
    55	// them. The human names power drift diagnosis: a stale-token rejection names
    56	// exactly which input(s) moved instead of listing six guesses.
    57	const TOKEN_FIELDS = /** @type {const} */ ([
    58	  ['generation', 'bundle generation'],
    59	  ['journalSeq', 'journal seq'],
    60	  ['handoffStatus', 'handoff status'],
    61	  ['receives', 'receive count'],
    62	  ['origin', 'intake origin'],
    63	  ['reason', 'intake reason'],
    64	  ['reasonClass', 'intake reason-class'],
    65	  ['configDigest', 'config'],
    66	  ['gitDigest', 'git state'],
    67	  ['probesDigest', 'probe cache'],
    68	  ['platform', 'target platform'],
    69	  ['sessionHint', 'target session'],
    70	]);
    71	const TOKEN_PREFIX = 'rcpt1';
    72	
    73	/**
    74	 * The revision fingerprint the receipt token derives from. Every field is a
    75	 * bound input: any drift between prepare and commit re-derives a different
    76	 * token, so a stale receipt can never commit (plan §Concurrency).
    77	 * @param {string} root @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
    78	 * @returns {Record<string, any>}
    79	 */
    80	function tokenInputs(root, bundle, opts, io) {
    81	  let configDigest = 'absent';
    82	  try {
    83	    configDigest = dedupeKey(io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/baton.config.json`, 'utf8'));
    84	  } catch {
    85	    // no config — 'absent' is itself a bound value
    86	  }
    87	  return {
    88	    generation: bundle.generation,
    89	    journalSeq: bundle.journalSeq,
    90	    // The seal state is a bound input too (gate-2 fix): a competing receiver
    91	    // that lands between this token's derivation and its commit changes
    92	    // status/receive_log, so the loser's token cannot re-derive.
    93	    handoffStatus: bundle.handoff?.status ?? null,
    94	    receives: Array.isArray(bundle.handoff?.receive_log) ? bundle.handoff.receive_log.length : 0,
    95	    origin: opts.origin,
    96	    reason: opts.reason,
    97	    // Explicit --reason-class is a bound intake input (gate-2 iter-2 M4): it
    98	    // changes the avoid[] decision, so a change between prepare and commit is
    99	    // drift that must re-prepare.
   100	    reasonClass: opts.reasonClass ?? null,
   101	    configDigest,
   102	    gitDigest: dedupeKey(opts.gitSnapshot ?? null),
   103	    probesDigest: dedupeKey(opts.probes ?? null),
   104	    platform: opts.platform,
   105	    sessionHint: opts.sessionHint,
   106	  };
   107	}
   108	
   109	/**
   110	 * Structured receipt token: a fixed-order join of per-input digests. Equality
   111	 * still gates commit exactly as the old opaque hash did; the structure only
   112	 * buys DIAGNOSIS — a mismatch can name the drifted input(s).
   113	 * @param {string} root @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   114	 */
   115	function deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io) {
   116	  const inputs = tokenInputs(root, bundle, opts, io);
   117	  return `${TOKEN_PREFIX}.` + TOKEN_FIELDS.map(([key]) => dedupeKey(inputs[key] ?? null).slice(0, 10)).join('.');
   118	}
   119	
   120	/**
   121	 * Name the inputs whose digests differ between two structured tokens.
   122	 * @param {string} presented @param {string} expected @returns {string[]}
   123	 */
   124	function driftedInputs(presented, expected) {
   125	  const a = presented.split('.');
   126	  const b = expected.split('.');
   127	  if (a[0] !== TOKEN_PREFIX || b[0] !== TOKEN_PREFIX || a.length !== b.length) return [];
   128	  return TOKEN_FIELDS.filter((_, i) => a[i + 1] !== b[i + 1]).map(([, name]) => name);
   129	}
   130	
   131	/**
   132	 * @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   133	 * @returns {string[]} staleness/degradation warnings
   134	 */
   135	function stalenessWarnings(bundle, opts, io) {
   136	  /** @type {string[]} */
   137	  const warnings = [];
   138	  if (bundle.handoff.status === 'open') {
   139	    warnings.push('source bundle is unsealed (open) — the handoff was never finalized; treating this as a degraded (limit-death) receive');
   140	  }
   141	  const ref = bundle.handoff.finalizedAt ?? bundle.updatedAt;
   142	  const ageMs = Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(ref);
   143	  if (Number.isFinite(ageMs) && ageMs > STALE_AFTER_MS) {
   144	    warnings.push(`bundle finalized ${Math.round(ageMs / 3_600_000)}h ago (staleness threshold 12h) — re-verify before continuing`);
   145	  }
   146	  const g = opts.gitSnapshot;
   147	  if (g && bundle.git) {
   148	    if (bundle.git.headSha !== g.headSha) {
   149	      warnings.push(`HEAD moved since capture: bundle recorded ${bundle.git.headSha}, working tree now ${g.headSha}`);
   150	    }
   151	    if (bundle.git.dirty !== g.dirty) {
   152	      warnings.push(`dirty-state mismatch: bundle captured dirty=${bundle.git.dirty}, working tree now dirty=${g.dirty}`);
   153	    }
   154	  }
   155	  return warnings;
   156	}
   157	
   158	/**
   159	 * Phase 1 of the receive transaction: validate, resolve roles, render the
   160	 * resume prompt, and return a one-time receipt token. Mutates NOTHING — the
   161	 * token is self-contained (a fingerprint hash), never a persisted record.
   162	 * @param {string} root @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   163	 * @returns {{token: string, prompt: string, assignments: Record<string, any>, warnings: string[]}}
   164	 */
   165	export function prepare(root, opts, io) {
   166	  const { bundle, warnings: loadWarnings } = loadBundle(root, io);
   167	  if (bundle === null) throw new Error(`no handoff bundle under ${root}/.handoff — nothing to receive`);
   168	  opts = effectiveIntake(bundle, opts);
   169	
   170	  const warnings = [...loadWarnings, ...stalenessWarnings(bundle, opts, io)];
   171	
   172	  const { config, errors } = loadConfig(root, io);
   173	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
   174	  let assignments = {};
   175	  if (config) {
   176	    // The dead origin comes from the BUNDLE first (its sealed reasonClass and
   177	    // recorded origin platform); typed intake only fills the gaps — a user
   178	    // should not need to retype the verbatim limit string to keep roles off
   179	    // the platform that died (gate-2 reviewer-b finding 4). Confidence gates
   180	    // auto-avoidance (gate-2 iter-2 M4): an explicit --reason-class or a sealed
   181	    // reasonClass is trusted; a live text classification only auto-avoids at
   182	    // medium+ confidence, else it warns and defers to explicit intake.
   183	    let reasonClass;
   184	    let confidence;
   185	    if (typeof opts.reasonClass === 'string') {
   186	      reasonClass = opts.reasonClass;
   187	      confidence = 'high'; // explicit user intake
   188	    } else if (typeof bundle.handoff?.reasonClass === 'string') {
   189	      reasonClass = bundle.handoff.reasonClass;
   190	      confidence = 'high'; // trusted seal
   191	    } else {
   192	      const v = classifyReason(opts.reason, opts.origin, io);
   193	      reasonClass = v.class;
   194	      confidence = v.confidence;
   195	    }
   196	    const deadOrigin =
   197	      typeof bundle.origin?.platform === 'string' && bundle.origin.platform !== 'unknown' ? bundle.origin.platform : opts.origin;
   198	    /** @type {string[]} */
   199	    let avoid = [];
   200	    if (reasonClass === 'usage-limit') {
   201	      if (confidence === 'low') {
   202	        warnings.push(
   203	          `the switch reason matched a usage-limit heuristic only at LOW confidence — not auto-avoiding ${deadOrigin}; pass --reason-class usage-limit to confirm the failover away from it`,
   204	        );
   205	      } else {
   206	        avoid = [deadOrigin];
   207	      }
   208	    }
   209	    assignments = resolveRoles({ config, to: opts.platform, avoid, nativeOnly: false, probes: opts.probes ?? null }).assignments;
   210	  } else {
   211	    warnings.push(`role matrix unavailable (${errors.map((e) => e.msg).join('; ')}) — no role table in this prompt`);
   212	  }
   213	
   214	  const prompt = renderResumePrompt({ bundle, assignments, warnings, origin: opts.origin, reason: opts.reason });
   215	  return { token: deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io), prompt, assignments, warnings };
   216	}
   217	
   218	/**
   219	 * Phase 2: one atomic transition. Re-derives every bound input from current
   220	 * disk state + opts and rejects ANY drift (throws, zero mutation). On match:
   221	 * writes the received seal, archives it via a 'receive' rotation, then opens a
   222	 * fresh writable generation owned by the receiving platform/session.
   223	 * @param {string} root @param {string} token @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   224	 * @returns {{adopted: boolean, generation: number, archivedTo: string | null, alreadyCommitted?: boolean}}
   225	 */
   226	export function commit(root, token, opts, io) {
   227	  // ONE operation-scoped lock across revalidation and every mutation (gate-2
   228	  // fix: reviewers a#1/b#11): two competing receivers serialize here, and the
   229	  // loser's re-derivation sees the winner's generation/status/receive_log.
   230	  return withLock(root, io, (fence) => commitLocked(root, token, opts, io, fence));
   231	}
   232	
   233	/**
   234	 * @param {string} root @param {string} token @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io @param {string} fence
   235	 * @returns {{adopted: boolean, generation: number, archivedTo: string | null, alreadyCommitted?: boolean}}
   236	 */
   237	function commitLocked(root, token, opts, io, fence) {
   238	  const { bundle } = loadBundle(root, io);
   239	  if (bundle === null) throw new Error(`no handoff bundle under ${root}/.handoff — nothing to receive; run receive --prepare first`);
   240	  opts = effectiveIntake(bundle, opts);
   241	
   242	  const expected = deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io);
   243	  if (expected !== token) {
   244	    // Idempotency first (audit finding: duplicate-receive corruption): if THIS
   245	    // exact receipt already landed — its digest is recorded on a receive_log
   246	    // entry — the retry is a lost-output re-send, not drift. Report success
   247	    // with zero mutation instead of sending the model into a re-prepare +
   248	    // re-commit loop that executes a second full receive.
   249	    const receipt = dedupeKey(token).slice(0, 16);
   250	    const landed = Array.isArray(bundle.handoff?.receive_log) && bundle.handoff.receive_log.some((/** @type {any} */ e) => e?.receipt === receipt);
   251	    if (landed) {
   252	      return { adopted: false, alreadyCommitted: true, generation: bundle.generation, archivedTo: null };
   253	    }
   254	    const drifted = driftedInputs(token, expected);
   255	    const detail = drifted.length > 0 ? `drifted input(s): ${drifted.join(', ')}` : 'a bound input drifted since prepare';
   256	    throw new Error(
   257	      `receipt token is stale — ${detail}. Re-prepare and retry, passing the SAME intake flags (--origin/--reason/--reason-class) to both prepare and --commit`,
   258	    );
   259	  }
   260	
   261	  const degraded = bundle.handoff.status !== 'sealed';
   262	  const receiveEntry = {
   263	    origin: opts.origin,
   264	    reason: opts.reason,
   265	    at: io.now(),
   266	    // The receipt digest keys the idempotent-retry detection above.
   267	    receipt: dedupeKey(token).slice(0, 16),
   268	    ...(degraded ? { degradedSeal: true } : {}),
   269	  };
   270	
   271	  // The degraded seal must NOT persist a LOW-confidence classification (iter-3
   272	  // F10): a sealed reasonClass is trusted as high-confidence by the next
   273	  // prepare, so laundering a low-confidence heuristic here would silently
   274	  // auto-avoid the origin one hop later. Explicit intake is high-confidence and
   275	  // seals; a live classification seals ONLY at medium+ confidence, else null
   276	  // (the next receive re-classifies the reason text at its true confidence).
   277	  let sealedReasonClass = null;
   278	  if (degraded) {
   279	    if (typeof opts.reasonClass === 'string') {
   280	      sealedReasonClass = opts.reasonClass;
   281	    } else {
   282	      const v = classifyReason(opts.reason, opts.origin, io);
   283	      sealedReasonClass = v.confidence === 'low' ? null : v.class;
   284	    }
   285	  }
   286	
   287	  const receivedSeal = {
   288	    ...bundle,
   289	    updatedAt: io.now(),
   290	    handoff: {
   291	      ...bundle.handoff,
   292	      status: 'received',
   293	      ...(degraded
   294	        ? {
   295	            reason: opts.reason,
   296	            reasonClass: sealedReasonClass,
   297	            finalizedAt: io.now(),
   298	          }
   299	        : {}),
   300	      receive_log: [...bundle.handoff.receive_log, receiveEntry],
   301	    },
   302	  };
   303	
   304	  // Freeze order: the received seal is written first so the 'receive' rotation
   305	  // archives exactly that state (never the pre-commit bundle). All three
   306	  // mutations run under the SAME lock acquisition, fence-checked per write.
   307	  writeSnapshotIn(root, receivedSeal, io, fence);
   308	  const archivedTo = rotateJournalIn(root, 'receive', io, fence);
   309	
   310	  const { config } = loadConfig(root, io);
   311	  const next = {
   312	    ...receivedSeal,
   313	    generation: bundle.generation + 1,
   314	    origin: {
   315	      platform: opts.platform,
   316	      model: config?.defaults?.[opts.platform] ?? 'unknown',
   317	      sessionHint: opts.sessionHint,
   318	      unstable: opts.sessionUnstable === true,
   319	    },
   320	    // The fresh writable generation has NO switch reason yet (iter-4 I3): the
   321	    // sealed reason/reasonClass/finalizedAt/toPlatformHint describe the PRIOR
   322	    // handoff, not this one. Carrying them forward made the next prepare trust a
   323	    // stale class as a high-confidence seal (silently auto-avoiding the healthy
   324	    // adopted origin) and poisoned both the 12 h staleness reference and the
   325	    // sessionStart limit-hit signal. Keep only receive_log (the audit chain).
   326	    handoff: { ...receivedSeal.handoff, status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, finalizedAt: null, toPlatformHint: null },
   327	  };
   328	  writeSnapshotIn(root, next, io, fence);
   329	
   330	  return { adopted: true, generation: next.generation, archivedTo };
   331	}

codex
The Layer-1 transaction facts are stricter than the plan’s prose in one place: receive tokens bind bundle/config/git/probe/session inputs, while loop-local state only matters if the loop also checkpoints it into the handoff bundle. I’m checking the supporting modules before deciding whether that is a plan defect or just a test wording issue.

     2	import { prepare, commit } from '../receive/txn.mjs';
     3	import { readProbeCache } from '../roles/availability.mjs';
     4	import { emitEnvelope, resolveRoot, usageError, parseFlagsStrict, platformError } from './shared.mjs';
     5	
     6	/**
     7	 * `baton receive` — the two-phase resume flow. `--print-prompt` and
     8	 * `--prepare` are read-only (prepare mutates nothing); `--commit <token>`
     9	 * performs the atomic transition. The receiving session hint defaults to a
    10	 * host-stable value so prepare and commit issued by separate CLI processes
    11	 * still derive the same token; adapters pass `--session` with their real id.
    12	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    13	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    14	 */
    15	export async function cmdReceive(args, io) {
    16	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string', origin: 'string', reason: 'string', 'reason-class': 'string', session: 'string', commit: 'string', prepare: 'boolean', 'print-prompt': 'boolean' });
    17	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'receive', parsed.error);
    18	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    19	  const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
    20	  if (!platform) return usageError(io, flags, 'receive', '--platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required');
    21	  const pErr = platformError(platform);
    22	  if (pErr) return usageError(io, flags, 'receive', pErr);
    23	
    24	  // Validate --reason-class against the class vocabulary BEFORE it is trusted as
    25	  // explicit intake and bound into the receipt token (iter-3 F11): finalize
    26	  // already does this, so receive must too — an out-of-enum class would seal a
    27	  // meaningless reasonClass verbatim.
    28	  const REASON_CLASSES = ['usage-limit', 'auth', 'throttle', 'other-error'];
    29	  if (flags['reason-class'] !== undefined && !REASON_CLASSES.includes(/** @type {string} */ (flags['reason-class']))) {
    30	    return usageError(io, flags, 'receive', `--reason-class must be one of ${REASON_CLASSES.join('|')}`);
    31	  }
    32	
    33	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    34	  const git = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
    35	  const hasSession = typeof flags.session === 'string';
    36	  const opts = {
    37	    platform,
    38	    origin: typeof flags.origin === 'string' ? flags.origin : 'unknown',
    39	    reason: typeof flags.reason === 'string' ? flags.reason : 'unspecified',
    40	    gitSnapshot: git,
    41	    // The fresh probe cache is a bound token input (probesDigest): remap uses
    42	    // it at prepare, and a cache change before commit is drift → re-prepare.
    43	    probes: readProbeCache(root, io),
    44	    // Explicit reason-class intake (gate-2 iter-2 M4): confirms a failover away
    45	    // from a low-confidence-classified origin; bound into the token.
    46	    ...(typeof flags['reason-class'] === 'string' ? { reasonClass: flags['reason-class'] } : {}),
    47	    sessionHint: hasSession ? /** @type {string} */ (flags.session) : `cli:${io.host}`,
    48	    // A defaulted host-derived hint is NOT the harness session id — mark it
    49	    // unstable so the receiving session's first real hook checkpoint adopts
    50	    // ownership instead of being rejected as foreign (gate-2 fix 2).
    51	    sessionUnstable: !hasSession,
    52	  };
    53	
    54	  // A lost commit token (empty shell var, --commit followed by another flag)
    55	  // must be a loud usage error — falling through to the read-only prepare path
    56	  // with exit 0 convinced models a commit landed when nothing mutated.
    57	  if (flags.commit !== undefined && typeof flags.commit !== 'string') {
    58	    return usageError(io, flags, 'receive', '--commit requires the receipt token printed by prepare (the token argument is missing)');
    59	  }
    60	  if (flags.commit !== undefined && flags.prepare !== undefined) {
    61	    return usageError(io, flags, 'receive', '--prepare and --commit are mutually exclusive phases');
    62	  }
    63	
    64	  if (typeof flags.commit === 'string') {
    65	    try {
    66	      const res = commit(root, flags.commit, opts, io);
    67	      if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: res });
    68	      else if (res.alreadyCommitted === true) io.stdout.write(`already committed — generation ${res.generation} is open on ${platform}; nothing to redo\n`);
    69	      else io.stdout.write(`received — generation ${res.generation} open on ${platform}; archived ${res.archivedTo}\n`);
    70	      return 0;
    71	    } catch (err) {
    72	      const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
    73	      if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'commit-rejected', msg } });
    74	      else io.stderr.write(`baton receive: ${msg}\n`);
    75	      return 1;
    76	    }
    77	  }
    78	
    79	  /** @type {ReturnType<typeof prepare>} */
    80	  let prepared;
    81	  try {
    82	    prepared = prepare(root, opts, io);
    83	  } catch (err) {
    84	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
    85	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'prepare-failed', msg } });
    86	    else io.stderr.write(`baton receive: ${msg}\n`);
    87	    return 1;
    88	  }
    89	
    90	  if (flags['print-prompt'] === true) {
    91	    // --json wraps the prompt in the envelope (gate-2 iter-2 M6); the bare
    92	    // form stays raw markdown — the adapter hook fallback path is unchanged.
    93	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { prompt: prepared.prompt, warnings: prepared.warnings } });
    94	    else io.stdout.write(prepared.prompt);
    95	    return 0;
    96	  }
    97	
    98	  if (flags.json) {
    99	    emitEnvelope(io, {
   100	      ok: true,
   101	      data: { token: prepared.token, prompt: prepared.prompt, assignments: prepared.assignments, warnings: prepared.warnings },
   102	    });
   103	  } else {
   104	    for (const w of prepared.warnings) io.stderr.write(`baton receive: ${w}\n`);
   105	    io.stderr.write(`baton receive: token ${prepared.token}\n`);
   106	    io.stdout.write(prepared.prompt);
   107	  }
   108	  return 0;
   109	}

     1	import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
     2	
     3	// Git facts degrade to null rather than block a checkpoint: not-a-repo,
     4	// missing binary, and timeouts are all expected environments (plan §Root
     5	// discovery & degraded modes).
     6	const GIT_TIMEOUT_MS = 5000;
     7	// The untracked digest is bounded: beyond this many paths, entries fold in by
     8	// name only, keeping prepare-time hashing cheap on huge untracked trees.
     9	const UNTRACKED_CAP = 200;
    10	// NUL separator: cannot occur in diffs, sizes, hex hashes, or file paths, so
    11	// joined digest inputs cannot collide across field boundaries.
    12	const SEP = String.fromCharCode(0);
    13	const NEWLINE = String.fromCharCode(10);
    14	
    15	const sha256 = (/** @type {string} */ s) => createHash('sha256').update(s).digest('hex');
    16	
    17	/**
    18	 * Capture the repo's git state plus a content-sensitive digest. The digest
    19	 * covers the staged diff, unstaged diff, and a bounded untracked-files digest
    20	 * (path + size + content hash) — NOT the HEAD sha, which the receive token
    21	 * binds as its own input, so a HEAD-only move is distinguishable from a
    22	 * content change.
    23	 * @param {{execFile: Function, cwd: string, fs: any}} deps
    24	 * @returns {Promise<{branch: string, headSha: string, dirty: boolean, dirtySummary: string[], contentDigest: string} | null>}
    25	 */
    26	export async function snapshot({ execFile, cwd, fs }) {
    27	  const git = (/** @type {string[]} */ args) => execFile('git', args, { cwd, timeout: GIT_TIMEOUT_MS });
    28	  let branch, head, status, staged, unstaged, untrackedList;
    29	  try {
    30	    [branch, head, status, staged, unstaged, untrackedList] = await Promise.all([
    31	      git(['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD']),
    32	      git(['rev-parse', 'HEAD']),
    33	      git(['status', '--porcelain']),
    34	      git(['diff', '--cached']),
    35	      git(['diff']),
    36	      git(['ls-files', '--others', '--exclude-standard']),
    37	    ]);
    38	  } catch {
    39	    return null;
    40	  }
    41	
    42	  const nonEmptyLines = (/** @type {string} */ s) => s.split(NEWLINE).filter((/** @type {string} */ l) => l !== '');
    43	  const dirtySummary = nonEmptyLines(status.stdout);
    44	  const untrackedPaths = nonEmptyLines(untrackedList.stdout);
    45	
    46	  const untrackedEntries = untrackedPaths.map((rel, i) => {
    47	    if (i >= UNTRACKED_CAP) return [rel, 'capped'].join(SEP);
    48	    try {
    49	      const content = fs.readFileSync(cwd + '/' + rel, 'utf8');
    50	      return [rel, String(Buffer.byteLength(content)), sha256(content)].join(SEP);
    51	    } catch {
    52	      // Listed but unreadable (deleted mid-flight): still digest its presence.
    53	      return [rel, 'absent'].join(SEP);
    54	    }
    55	  });
    56	
    57	  return {
    58	    branch: branch.stdout.trim(),
    59	    headSha: head.stdout.trim(),
    60	    dirty: dirtySummary.length > 0,
    61	    dirtySummary,
    62	    contentDigest: sha256([staged.stdout, unstaged.stdout, ...untrackedEntries].join(SEP)),
    63	  };
    64	}

     1	const PRECEDENCE = ['usage-limit', 'auth', 'throttle', 'other-error'];
     2	
     3	/** @type {Record<string, string>} */
     4	const STOP_FAILURE_MAP = {
     5	  rate_limit: 'usage-limit',
     6	  overloaded: 'throttle',
     7	  authentication_failed: 'auth',
     8	  oauth_org_not_allowed: 'auth',
     9	  billing_error: 'auth',
    10	};

    12	// eslint-disable-next-line no-control-regex
    13	const ANSI = /\x1b\[[0-9;]*[A-Za-z]/g;
    14	
    15	/** @param {any} matcher @param {string} text */
    16	function matches(matcher, text) {
    17	  switch (matcher.kind) {
    18	    case 'substring':
    19	      return text.includes(matcher.value);
    20	    case 'regex':
    21	      return new RegExp(matcher.pattern, matcher.flags || '').test(text);
    22	    case 'json-field': {
    23	      for (const line of text.split('\n')) {
    24	        let obj;
    25	        try {
    26	          obj = JSON.parse(line);
    27	        } catch {
    28	          continue;
    29	        }
    30	        const val = String(matcher.path)
    31	          .split('.')
    32	          .reduce((o, k) => (o == null ? undefined : o[k]), obj);
    33	        if (val === matcher.equals) return true;
    34	      }
    35	      return false;
    36	    }
    37	    default:
    38	      return false;
    39	  }
    40	}
    41	
    42	/**
    43	 * Classify harness output. Structured evidence first (text and exit code are
    44	 * ignored when a recognized structured signal is present); text signatures as
    45	 * the fallback tier; exit code only breaks the no-match tie — a generic nonzero
    46	 * exit is never usage-limit on its own.
    47	 * @param {{text?: string, exitCode?: number, platform: string, table: any, structured?: {kind: string, errorType: string} | null}} input
    48	 * @returns {{class: string, signatureId: string | null, confidence: string | null, resetHint: string | null, tried?: string[]}}
    49	 */
    50	export function classify({ text = '', exitCode = 0, platform, table, structured = null }) {
    51	  if (structured) {
    52	    if (structured.kind === 'stop-failure') {
    53	      const cls = STOP_FAILURE_MAP[structured.errorType] ?? 'other-error';
    54	      return { class: cls, signatureId: `structured:${structured.errorType}`, confidence: 'high', resetHint: null };
    55	    }
    56	    if (structured.kind === 'exec-json') {
    57	      const cls = structured.errorType === 'usage_limit' ? 'usage-limit' : 'other-error';
    58	      return { class: cls, signatureId: `structured:${structured.errorType}`, confidence: 'high', resetHint: null };
    59	    }
    60	    // Unrecognized structured kind: fall through to the text tier.
    61	  }
    62	
    63	  const stripped = text.replace(ANSI, '');
    64	  const candidates = table.signatures.filter((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.platform === platform);
    65	  /** @type {any} */
    66	  let best = null;
    67	  for (const s of candidates) {
    68	    if (!matches(s.matcher, stripped)) continue;
    69	    if (best === null || PRECEDENCE.indexOf(s.class) < PRECEDENCE.indexOf(best.class)) best = s;
    70	  }
    71	
    72	  if (best) {
    73	    let resetHint = null;
    74	    if (typeof best.resetHint === 'string') {
    75	      const m = stripped.match(new RegExp(best.resetHint));
    76	      if (m) resetHint = m[1] ?? m[0];
    77	    }
    78	    return { class: best.class, signatureId: best.id, confidence: best.confidence, resetHint };
    79	  }
    80	
    81	  return {
    82	    class: exitCode !== 0 ? 'other-error' : 'ok',
    83	    signatureId: null,
    84	    confidence: null,
    85	    resetHint: null,
    86	  };
    87	}

   201	/**
   202	 * Classify one probe invocation per the two-dimension record: capability is
   203	 * the ordered ladder verified as far as the evidence goes; outcome is what the
   204	 * probe itself did. A server-issued limit response proves authentication.
   205	 * @param {{platform: string, bin: string, args: string[], capOnSuccess?: string}} probe @param {any} io @param {any} table
   206	 */
   207	async function runProbe(probe, io, table) {
   208	  try {
   209	    await io.execFile(probe.bin, probe.args, { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
   210	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: probe.capOnSuccess ?? 'reachable', outcome: 'ok' };
   211	  } catch (err) {
   212	    const e = /** @type {any} */ (err);
   213	    if (e?.code === 'ENOENT') return { platform: probe.platform, capability: null, outcome: 'error' };
   214	    if (e?.killed === true) return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'timeout' };
   215	    const text = `${e?.stdout ?? ''}\n${e?.stderr ?? ''}`;
   216	    if (table && classify({ text, exitCode: 1, platform: probe.platform, table, structured: null }).class === 'usage-limit') {
   217	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'rate-limited' };
   218	    }
   219	    if (/authenticated as|logged in as/i.test(text) && /unreachable|ECONN|offline|network/i.test(text)) {
   220	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'error' };
   221	    }
   222	    if (/not logged in|log ?in first|unauthorized|authenticat/i.test(text)) {
   223	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   224	    }
   225	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   226	  }
   227	}

   246	      };
   247	    }
   248	  }
   249	  return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: `no AI trailers in the last ${GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND} commits` };

     1	/**
     2	 * Load + validate baton.config.json, parsing role chains from their
     3	 * "platform/model[@effort]" string form into structured entries.
     4	 * Never throws: returns {config, errors} with dotted-path errors.
     5	 * @param {string} cwd @param {any} io
     6	 * @returns {{config: any | null, errors: {path: string, msg: string}[]}}
     7	 */
     8	export function loadConfig(cwd, io) {
     9	  const file = `${cwd}/baton.config.json`;
    10	  /** @type {{path: string, msg: string}[]} */
    11	  const errors = [];
    12	
    13	  let raw;
    14	  try {
    15	    raw = io.fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8');
    16	  } catch {
    17	    return { config: null, errors: [{ path: '', msg: `baton.config.json not found at ${file} — run 'baton init' to scaffold it` }] };
    18	  }
    19	  let parsed;
    20	  try {
    21	    parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
    22	  } catch (err) {
    23	    return { config: null, errors: [{ path: '', msg: `baton.config.json is not valid JSON (${/** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? 'parse error'})` }] };
    24	  }
    25	
    26	  if (parsed.roles === null || typeof parsed.roles !== 'object' || Array.isArray(parsed.roles)) {
    27	    errors.push({ path: 'roles', msg: 'roles must be an object mapping role names to chain arrays' });
    28	  }
    29	
    30	  /** @type {Record<string, any[]>} */
    31	  const roles = {};
    32	  if (errors.length === 0) {
    33	    for (const [role, chain] of Object.entries(parsed.roles)) {
    34	      if (!Array.isArray(chain)) {
    35	        errors.push({ path: `roles.${role}`, msg: 'a role chain must be an array of "platform/model[@effort]" strings' });
    36	        continue;
    37	      }
    38	      /** @type {any[]} */
    39	      const entries = [];
    40	      for (let i = 0; i < chain.length; i += 1) {
    41	        const s = chain[i];
    42	        const slash = typeof s === 'string' ? s.indexOf('/') : -1;
    43	        if (slash <= 0 || slash === s.length - 1) {
    44	          errors.push({ path: `roles.${role}[${i}]`, msg: `malformed chain entry ${JSON.stringify(s)} — expected "platform/model[@effort]"` });
    45	          continue;
    46	        }
    47	        const platform = s.slice(0, slash);
    48	        const rest = s.slice(slash + 1);
    49	        const at = rest.indexOf('@');
    50	        const model = at === -1 ? rest : rest.slice(0, at);
    51	        const effort = at === -1 ? null : rest.slice(at + 1);
    52	        if (parsed.platforms && !(platform in parsed.platforms)) {
    53	          errors.push({ path: `roles.${role}[${i}]`, msg: `unknown platform "${platform}" — not a key of config.platforms` });
    54	          continue;
    55	        }
    56	        entries.push({ platform, model, effort });
    57	      }
    58	      roles[role] = entries;
    59	    }
    60	  }
    61	
    62	  if (errors.length > 0) return { config: null, errors };
    63	  return { config: { ...parsed, roles }, errors };
    64	}

    12	/**
    13	 * Infer the reason class by running the reason text through the classifier
    14	 * against the bundle's origin platform. No signature match reads as 'ok',
    15	 * which is not a switch reason — degrade to null rather than mislabel.
    16	 * @param {string} reason @param {string} platform @param {any} io
    17	 * @returns {string | null}
    18	 */
    19	function inferReasonClass(reason, platform, io) {
    20	  try {
    21	    const table = loadSignatures({ builtinPath: BUILTIN_SIGNATURES }, io);
    22	    const verdict = classify({ text: reason, exitCode: 0, platform, table, structured: null });
    23	    return verdict.class === 'ok' ? null : verdict.class;
    24	  } catch {
    25	    return null;
    26	  }
    27	}

    29	/**
    30	 * `baton finalize` — the narrative seal: records the switch reason, refreshes
    31	 * git state, seals the bundle, and freezes this generation into history via a
    32	 * 'finalize' journal rotation.
    33	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    34	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    35	 */
    36	export async function cmdFinalize(args, io) {
    37	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { reason: 'string', 'reason-class': 'string', to: 'string' });
    38	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'finalize', parsed.error);
    39	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    40	  const reason = typeof flags.reason === 'string' ? flags.reason : null;
    41	  if (!reason) return usageError(io, flags, 'finalize', '--reason "<why you are switching>" is required');
    42	  const toErr = platformError(flags.to);
    43	  if (toErr) return usageError(io, flags, 'finalize', `--to: ${toErr}`);
    44	  const REASON_CLASSES = ['usage-limit', 'auth', 'throttle', 'other-error'];
    45	  if (flags['reason-class'] !== undefined && !REASON_CLASSES.includes(/** @type {string} */ (flags['reason-class']))) {
    46	    return usageError(io, flags, 'finalize', `--reason-class must be one of ${REASON_CLASSES.join('|')}`);
    47	  }
    48	
    49	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    50	  const paths = bundlePaths(root);
    51	  const { bundle, warnings } = loadBundle(root, io);
    52	  for (const w of warnings) io.stderr.write(`baton finalize: ${w}\n`);
    53	  if (bundle === null) {
    54	    const error = { code: 'no-bundle', msg: `no handoff bundle at ${paths.snapshot}; nothing to finalize` };
    55	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error });
    56	    else io.stderr.write(`baton finalize: ${error.msg}\n`);
    57	    return 1;
    58	  }
    59	
    60	  // Capture git BEFORE the lock — it is async and must not straddle the lock.
    61	  const git = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
    62	  if (git === null) io.stderr.write('baton finalize: git state unavailable — sealing with git marked unavailable (not the stale prior snapshot)\n');
    63	
    64	  // ONE atomic locked span (iter-5 A2): the seal, snapshot write, and journal
    65	  // rotation run under a SINGLE lock acquisition + fence, and the bundle is
    66	  // RELOADED inside the lock. The old code loaded before the async git await
    67	  // then sealed + wrote + rotated through separate locks, so a decision appended
    68	  // during the await was sealed from a stale bundle and survived only in the
    69	  // rotated journal. Reloading inside the lock includes it in the seal.
    70	  const sealed = withLock(root, io, (token) => {
    71	    const current = loadBundle(root, io).bundle;
    72	    if (current === null) return null; // deleted concurrently between the checks
    73	    const reasonClass =
    74	      typeof flags['reason-class'] === 'string' ? flags['reason-class'] : inferReasonClass(reason, current.origin.platform, io);
    75	    const s = {
    76	      ...current,
    77	      // NEVER seal the stale prior git snapshot (iter-4 I2 sibling): a receive
    78	      // audits the seal's git against live state, so stale HEAD/dirty would emit
    79	      // false "HEAD moved"/"dirty mismatch" warnings. On refresh failure seal an
    80	      // explicit unavailable (null) git; receive re-derives it live regardless.
    81	      git,
    82	      updatedAt: io.now(),
    83	      handoff: {
    84	        ...current.handoff,
    85	        status: 'sealed',
    86	        reason,
    87	        reasonClass,
    88	        toPlatformHint: typeof flags.to === 'string' ? flags.to : null,
    89	        finalizedAt: io.now(),
    90	      },
    91	    };
    92	    writeSnapshotIn(root, s, io, token);
    93	    rotateJournalIn(root, 'finalize', io, token);
    94	    return s;
    95	  });
    96	
    97	  if (sealed === null) {
    98	    const error = { code: 'no-bundle', msg: `handoff bundle vanished during finalize at ${paths.snapshot}` };
    99	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error });
   100	    else io.stderr.write(`baton finalize: ${error.msg}\n`);
   101	    return 1;
   102	  }
   103	
   104	  // Inference only matches verbatim harness banners; a natural paraphrase
   105	  // seals reasonClass:null — and receive's role remap only auto-avoids the dead
   106	  // platform on 'usage-limit'. Surface the gap instead of silently defeating
   107	  // the failover heuristics (audit finding).
   108	  if (sealed.handoff.reasonClass === null && flags['reason-class'] === undefined) {
   109	    io.stderr.write(
   110	      'baton finalize: no reason class inferred from the reason text — if this switch was forced, re-run with --reason-class <usage-limit|throttle|auth|other-error> so receive keeps roles off the exhausted platform\n',
   111	    );
   112	  }
   113	
   114	  if (flags.json) {
   115	    emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { bundlePath: paths.snapshot, handoffMdPath: paths.handoffMd } });
   116	  } else {
   117	    const rc = sealed.handoff.reasonClass;
   118	    io.stdout.write(`sealed — reason: ${reason}${rc ? ` (${rc})` : ''}${sealed.handoff.toPlatformHint ? ` — next: ${sealed.handoff.toPlatformHint}` : ''}\n`);
   119	  }
   120	  return 0;

     8	/**
     9	 * Is a quantified group's body ambiguous? Alternation, another quantifier, or
    10	 * any nested group inside a `(…)+`/`(…)*`/`(…){` makes backtracking
    11	 * super-linear in the worst case — conservatively rejected (gate-2 fix 6:
    12	 * the old lint accepted `(a|aa)+$` and the `(a|a)*x` live repro).
    13	 * @param {string} body
    14	 */
    15	function riskyGroupBody(body) {
    16	  // Strip a non-capture/lookaround/named marker so `(?:abc)+` reads as `abc`.
    17	  const b = body.replace(/^\?(?:<=|<!|:|=|!|<[^>]*>)/, '');
    18	  let inClass = false;
    19	  for (let i = 0; i < b.length; i++) {
    20	    const c = b[i];
    21	    if (c === '\\') {
    22	      i++;
    23	      continue;
    24	    }
    25	    if (inClass) {
    26	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
    27	      continue;
    28	    }
    29	    if (c === '[') {
    30	      inClass = true;
    31	      continue;
    32	    }
    33	    if (c === '|' || c === '+' || c === '*' || c === '{' || c === '?' || c === '(' || c === ')') return true;
    34	  }
    35	  return false;
    36	}
    37	
    38	/**
    39	 * Structural scan: walk the pattern (class- and escape-aware), and for every
    40	 * group followed by an unbounded quantifier check its full body — any nesting
    41	 * depth — for ambiguity. Returns a problem string or null.
    42	 * @param {string} pattern
    43	 */
    44	function scanQuantifiedGroups(pattern) {
    45	  /** @type {number[]} */
    46	  const opens = [];
    47	  let inClass = false;
    48	  for (let i = 0; i < pattern.length; i++) {
    49	    const c = pattern[i];
    50	    if (c === '\\') {
    51	      i++;
    52	      continue;
    53	    }
    54	    if (inClass) {
    55	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
    56	      continue;
    57	    }
    58	    if (c === '[') {
    59	      inClass = true;
    60	      continue;
    61	    }
    62	    if (c === '(') {
    63	      opens.push(i);
    64	      continue;
    65	    }
    66	    if (c === ')') {
    67	      const start = opens.pop();
    68	      if (start === undefined) continue; // unbalanced — new RegExp() reports it
    69	      const next = pattern[i + 1];
    70	      if (next === '+' || next === '*' || next === '{') {
    71	        const body = pattern.slice(start + 1, i);
    72	        if (riskyGroupBody(body)) {
    73	          return `quantified group "(${body})" contains alternation, another quantifier, or a nested group`;
    74	        }
    75	      }
    76	    }
    77	  }
    78	  return null;
    79	}
    80	
    81	/**
    82	 * Approximate character-set overlap between two simple-atom alphabets.
    83	 * Descriptors: {all:true} (dot / broad negated shorthand) | {sh:'d'|'w'|'s'} |
    84	 * {lit:Set<string>}. Returns true unless the two sets are PROVABLY disjoint —
    85	 * conservative for untrusted overlays (iter-4 I4).
    86	 * @param {any} a @param {any} b
    87	 */
    88	function alphasOverlap(a, b) {
    89	  if (!a || !b) return false;
    90	  if (a.all || b.all) return true;
    91	  if (a.sh && b.sh) {
    92	    if (a.sh === b.sh) return true;
    93	    // \s is disjoint from \d and \w; \d ⊂ \w so they overlap.
    94	    return !(a.sh === 's' || b.sh === 's');
    95	  }
    96	  if (a.lit && b.lit) {
    97	    for (const c of a.lit) if (b.lit.has(c)) return true;
    98	    return false;
    99	  }
   100	  const sh = a.sh ? a.sh : b.sh;
   101	  const lit = a.lit ? a.lit : b.lit;
   102	  for (const c of lit) {
   103	    if (sh === 'd' && c >= '0' && c <= '9') return true;
   104	    if (sh === 'w' && /[A-Za-z0-9_]/.test(c)) return true;
   105	    if (sh === 's' && /\s/.test(c)) return true;
   106	  }
   107	  return false;
   108	}
   109	
   110	/**
   111	 * Structural scan for a run of ≥2 CONSECUTIVE atoms, each with an UNBOUNDED
   112	 * quantifier (`*`, `+`, `{n,}`), whose alphabets overlap — `.*.*…`, `\d*\d*…`,
   113	 * `\(*\(*…`, `\n*\n*…`, `[a-z]*[a-z]*…`. Such a chain partitions the same input
   114	 * combinatorially and backtracks super-linearly, yet is invisible to the
   115	 * quantified-GROUP scan; and because a finite probe alphabet cannot guarantee a
   116	 * non-matching tail for every atom (`.` accepts almost everything), rejecting
   117	 * the STRUCTURE at load is the reliable guard (iter-4 I4, reviewer-a #7 /
   118	 * reviewer-b #3). Groups reset the run (the group scan covers quantified groups;
   119	 * a group's overlap with a simple atom is not decided here). Returns a problem
   120	 * string or null.
   121	 * @param {string} pattern
   122	 * @returns {string | null}
   123	 */
   124	function scanChainedQuantifiedAtoms(pattern) {
   125	  const p = pattern;
   126	  /** @type {any} */
   127	  let prev = null; // alpha of the previous atom IF it carried an unbounded quantifier, else null
   128	
   129	  for (let i = 0; i < p.length; ) {
   130	    const c = p[i];
   131	    // Chain-breakers: alternation and anchors end any run.
   132	    if (c === '|' || c === '^' || c === '$') {
   133	      prev = null;
   134	      i += 1;
   135	      continue;
   136	    }
   137	    /** @type {any} */
   138	    let alpha = null;
   139	    let isGroup = false;
   140	    if (c === '\\') {
   141	      const n = p[i + 1];
   142	      // decode length of the escape token
   143	      let len = 2;
   144	      if (n === 'x') len = 4;
   145	      else if (n === 'u') len = p[i + 2] === '{' ? (p.indexOf('}', i) - i + 1) : 6;
   146	      if (n === 'd' || n === 'w' || n === 's') alpha = { sh: n };
   147	      else if (n === 'D' || n === 'W' || n === 'S' || n === 'b' || n === 'B') alpha = { all: true }; // broad/negated or anchor-ish → conservative
   148	      else alpha = { lit: new Set([decodeEscape(p.slice(i, i + len))]) }; // \(, \n, \t, \xNN, \uNNNN → the literal char
   149	      i += Math.max(2, len);
   150	    } else if (c === '[') {
   151	      const end = classEnd(p, i);
   152	      const body = p.slice(i + 1, end);
   153	      alpha = body.startsWith('^') ? { all: true } : classAlpha(body);
   154	      i = end + 1;
   155	    } else if (c === '(') {
   156	      // Skip the whole group (balance-aware); recurse into its body for chains.
   157	      const end = groupEnd(p, i);
   158	      const inner = scanChainedQuantifiedAtoms(p.slice(i + 1, end));
   159	      if (inner) return inner;
   160	      isGroup = true;
   161	      i = end + 1;
   162	    } else if (c === '.') {
   163	      alpha = { all: true };
   164	      i += 1;
   165	    } else {
   166	      alpha = { lit: new Set([c]) };
   167	      i += 1;
   168	    }
   169	
   170	    // Read an optional quantifier and classify it as unbounded or not.
   171	    let unbounded = false;
   172	    const q = p[i];
   173	    if (q === '*' || q === '+') {
   174	      unbounded = true;
   175	      i += 1;
   176	    } else if (q === '{') {
   177	      const close = p.indexOf('}', i);
   178	      if (close !== -1) {
   179	        const spec = p.slice(i + 1, close);
   180	        // {n,} (no upper bound) is unbounded; {n} and {n,m} are bounded.
   181	        unbounded = /^\d+,\s*$/.test(spec);
   182	        i = close + 1;
   183	      }
   184	    } else if (q === '?') {
   185	      i += 1;
   186	    }
   187	    // consume a lazy/possessive modifier if present
   188	    if (p[i] === '?' || p[i] === '+') i += 1;
   189	
   190	    if (isGroup) {
   191	      // A group breaks the simple-atom run.
   192	      prev = null;
   193	      continue;
   194	    }
   195	    // prev + current = a run of ≥2 CONSECUTIVE overlapping unbounded-quantified
   196	    // atoms → super-linear backtracking. Reject.
   197	    if (unbounded && prev && alphasOverlap(prev, alpha)) {
   198	      return 'chained overlapping unbounded quantifiers (e.g. `.*.*`, `\\d*\\d*`, `\\(*\\(*`) backtrack super-linearly';
   199	    }
   200	    prev = unbounded ? alpha : null;
   201	  }
   202	  return null;
   203	}
   204	
   205	/** Decode a single-char escape token (`\(`, `\n`, `\t`, `\xNN`, `\uNNNN`, `\u{...}`) to its literal char. @param {string} tok */
   206	function decodeEscape(tok) {
   207	  const rest = tok.slice(1);
   208	  if (rest[0] === 'x') return String.fromCharCode(parseInt(rest.slice(1, 3), 16));
   209	  if (rest[0] === 'u') {
   210	    const h = rest[1] === '{' ? rest.slice(2, rest.indexOf('}')) : rest.slice(1, 5);
   211	    try {
   212	      return String.fromCodePoint(parseInt(h, 16));
   213	    } catch {
   214	      return rest[0];
   215	    }
   216	  }
   217	  const named = { n: '\n', t: '\t', r: '\r', f: '\f', v: '\v', 0: '\0' };
   218	  return named[/** @type {'n'} */ (rest[0])] ?? rest[0];
   219	}
   220	
   221	/** Index of the `]` closing a class opened at `open` (a `]` immediately after `[`/`[^` is a literal). @param {string} p @param {number} open */
   222	function classEnd(p, open) {
   223	  let i = open + 1;
   224	  if (p[i] === '^') i += 1;
   225	  if (p[i] === ']') i += 1; // leading ] is literal
   226	  for (; i < p.length; i++) {
   227	    if (p[i] === '\\') {
   228	      i += 1;
   229	      continue;
   230	    }
   231	    if (p[i] === ']') return i;
   232	  }
   233	  return p.length - 1;
   234	}
   235	
   236	/** Index of the `)` closing a group opened at `open` (balance-aware, class/escape-safe). @param {string} p @param {number} open */
   237	function groupEnd(p, open) {
   238	  let depth = 0;
   239	  let inClass = false;
   240	  for (let i = open; i < p.length; i++) {
   241	    const c = p[i];
   242	    if (c === '\\') {
   243	      i += 1;
   244	      continue;
   245	    }
   246	    if (inClass) {
   247	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
   248	      continue;
   249	    }
   250	    if (c === '[') inClass = true;
   251	    else if (c === '(') depth += 1;
   252	    else if (c === ')') {
   253	      depth -= 1;
   254	      if (depth === 0) return i;
   255	    }
   256	  }
   257	  return p.length - 1;
   258	}
   259	
   260	/** Approximate alphabet of a (non-negated) class body: literals + range endpoints + shorthands. @param {string} body */
   261	function classAlpha(body) {
   262	  if (/\\[dwsDWS]/.test(body) || /\\[DWS]/.test(body)) return { all: true }; // shorthand inside a class → treat broadly
   263	  /** @type {Set<string>} */
   264	  const lit = new Set();
   265	  for (let i = 0; i < body.length; i++) {
   266	    if (body[i] === '\\') {
   267	      lit.add(decodeEscape(body.slice(i, i + 2)));
   268	      i += 1;
   269	      continue;
   270	    }
   271	    // range a-z: add endpoints (a run of an endpoint char backtracks the same)
   272	    if (body[i + 1] === '-' && body[i + 2] !== undefined) {
   273	      lit.add(body[i]);
   274	      lit.add(body[i + 2]);
   275	      i += 2;
   276	      continue;
   277	    }
   278	    lit.add(body[i]);
   279	  }
   280	  return { lit };
   281	}
   282	
   283	const ALLOWED_FLAGS = 'dgimsuvy';
   284	
   285	/**
   286	 * Validate a regex flags string (gate-2 iter-2 B3): every char must be an
   287	 * allowed JS flag with no duplicates, so a flags value that compiles nowhere
   288	 * (`z`, `ii`) is rejected at LOAD, not left to throw later inside classify().
   289	 * @param {string} id @param {string} flags
   290	 */
   291	function lintFlags(id, flags) {
   292	  if (typeof flags !== 'string') throw new Error(`signature ${id}: regex flags must be a string`);
   293	  const seen = new Set();
   294	  for (const c of flags) {
   295	    if (!ALLOWED_FLAGS.includes(c)) throw new Error(`signature ${id}: unsupported regex flag "${c}" (allowed: ${ALLOWED_FLAGS})`);
   296	    if (seen.has(c)) throw new Error(`signature ${id}: duplicate regex flag "${c}"`);
   297	    seen.add(c);
   298	  }
   299	}
   300	
   301	/**
   302	 * Lint one regex pattern together with its flags. With the load-time worker
   303	 * probe on overlay tables (see probe.mjs), the runtime time-guard is by
   304	 * construction: patterns that could backtrack catastrophically never reach
   305	 * classify(), which stays synchronous with no timers.
   306	 * @param {string} id @param {string} pattern @param {string} [flags]
   307	 */
   308	function lintRegex(id, pattern, flags = '') {
   309	  if (typeof pattern !== 'string' || pattern.length > REGEX_MAX_LEN) {
   310	    throw new Error(`signature ${id}: regex pattern exceeds the ${REGEX_MAX_LEN}-char length cap (or is not a string)`);
   311	  }
   312	  if (/\\[1-9]/.test(pattern)) {
   313	    throw new Error(`signature ${id}: regex backreferences are not allowed`);
   314	  }
   315	  lintFlags(id, flags);
   316	  const problem = scanQuantifiedGroups(pattern) || scanChainedQuantifiedAtoms(pattern);
   317	  if (problem) {
   318	    throw new Error(`signature ${id}: ${problem} (pathological backtracking / non-linear-time risk)`);
   319	  }
   320	  try {
   321	    // eslint-disable-next-line no-new
   322	    new RegExp(pattern, flags);
   323	  } catch (err) {
   324	    throw new Error(`signature ${id}: invalid regex pattern/flags (${/** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? 'syntax error'})`);
   325	  }
   326	}
   327	
   328	/** @param {any} s */
   329	function lintSignature(s) {
   330	  if (typeof s.id !== 'string' || s.id.length === 0) throw new Error('signature with missing id');
   331	  if (!PLATFORMS.includes(s.platform)) {
   332	    throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: unknown platform "${s.platform}" (expected ${PLATFORMS.join('|')})`);
   333	  }
   334	  if (!CLASSES.includes(s.class)) throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: unknown class "${s.class}"`);
   335	  if (!s.matcher || !MATCHER_KINDS.includes(s.matcher.kind)) {
   336	    throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: unknown matcher kind "${s.matcher?.kind}" (expected ${MATCHER_KINDS.join('|')})`);
   337	  }
   338	  if (s.matcher.kind === 'regex') lintRegex(s.id, s.matcher.pattern, s.matcher.flags ?? '');
   339	  if (s.matcher.kind === 'substring' && typeof s.matcher.value !== 'string') {
   340	    throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: substring matcher needs a string value`);
   341	  }
   342	  if (s.matcher.kind === 'json-field' && typeof s.matcher.path !== 'string') {
   343	    throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: json-field matcher needs a dotted string path`);
   344	  }
   345	  if (s.resetHint !== undefined) lintRegex(s.id, s.resetHint);
   346	}
   347	
   348	/** @param {any} fs @param {string} path @param {{probe?: boolean}} [opts] */
   349	function readTable(fs, path, opts = {}) {
   350	  const raw = fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
   351	  const table = JSON.parse(raw);
   352	  if (table?.schema !== 'baton/signatures@1') {
   353	    throw new Error(`signature table at ${path}: expected schema baton/signatures@1, got ${JSON.stringify(table?.schema)}`);
   354	  }
   355	  if (!Array.isArray(table.signatures)) throw new Error(`signature table at ${path}: signatures must be an array`);
   356	  for (const s of table.signatures) {
   357	    lintSignature(s);
   358	    // Overlay tables are untrusted: the structural lint cannot see group-free
   359	    // pathological shapes (chained stars), so every overlay regex additionally
   360	    // runs against adversarial inputs in a worker with a hard deadline.
   361	    if (opts.probe === true) {
   362	      for (const pattern of [s.matcher?.kind === 'regex' ? s.matcher.pattern : null, s.resetHint ?? null]) {
   363	        if (typeof pattern !== 'string') continue;
   364	        const verdict = probeRegexSafe(pattern, s.matcher?.flags ?? '');
   365	        if (!verdict.safe) throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: ${verdict.reason}`);
   366	      }
   367	    }
   368	  }
   369	  return table;
   370	}
   371	
   372	/**
   373	 * Load + validate the signature table, merging an optional overlay by id
   374	 * (later wins — the drift-absorbing hot-patch surface). Builtin patterns are
   375	 * package-shipped and statically linted; overlay patterns are additionally
   376	 * probe-guarded at load.
   377	 * @param {{builtinPath: string, overlayPath?: string}} paths @param {any} io
   378	 */
   379	export function loadSignatures({ builtinPath, overlayPath }, io) {
   380	  const base = readTable(io.fs, builtinPath);
   381	  if (!overlayPath) return base;
   382	
   383	  const overlay = readTable(io.fs, overlayPath, { probe: true });
   384	  const merged = [...base.signatures];
   385	  for (const s of overlay.signatures) {
   386	    const i = merged.findIndex((b) => b.id === s.id);
   387	    if (i === -1) merged.push(s);
   388	    else merged[i] = s;
   389	  }
   390	  return { ...base, updated: overlay.updated ?? base.updated, signatures: merged };
   391	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	import { hostname } from 'node:os';
     2	
     3	// Best-effort extraction of baton events from raw harness hook payloads.
     4	// Total: any input value yields an Event[] — garbage degrades to note events,
     5	// never a throw, so a checkpoint can never break the host harness.
     6	
     7	/** @typedef {{host: string, pid: number, startTime: number | null}} SessionFacts */
     8	
     9	/** @type {Record<string, string>} */
    10	const FILE_TOOL_OPS = { Write: 'write', Edit: 'edit' };
    11	
    12	/** @returns {SessionFacts} */
    13	function processFacts() {
    14	  return { host: hostname(), pid: process.pid, startTime: Math.round(performance.timeOrigin) };
    15	}
    16	
    17	/**
    18	 * A process-scoped hint for payloads lacking a stable session id. Deterministic
    19	 * over the identity facts: one process => one hint; unstable hints never
    20	 * trigger foreign-session rejection on their own (enforced at the command).
    21	 * @param {SessionFacts | undefined} session
    22	 */
    23	function generatedHint(session) {
    24	  const f = session ?? processFacts();
    25	  return ['unstable', f.host, f.pid, f.startTime].join(':');
    26	}
    27	
    28	/**
    29	 * @param {any} raw
    30	 * @param {SessionFacts | undefined} session
    31	 * @returns {{sessionHint: string, unstable: boolean}}
    32	 */
    33	function deriveHint(raw, session) {
    34	  // Cursor identifies the session as conversation_id (audit finding 26): with
    35	  // only session_id read, every cursor hook fire fell back to a per-process
    36	  // unstable hint — a warning on every invocation and ownership adoption that
    37	  // never engaged.
    38	  const id = raw && typeof raw === 'object' ? (raw.session_id ?? raw.conversation_id) : undefined;
    39	  if (typeof id === 'string' && id.length > 0) return { sessionHint: id, unstable: false };
    40	  return { sessionHint: generatedHint(session), unstable: true };
    41	}
    42	
    43	/**
    44	 * Normalize a parsed hook payload into baton/event@1 events. Adapters that
    45	 * already speak baton/event@1 pass through verbatim; raw per-harness payloads
    46	 * get best-effort extraction; unknowns degrade to note events. Never throws.
    47	 * @param {any} raw parsed JS value (the caller owns JSON.parse)
    48	 * @param {string} platform 'claude-code' | 'codex' | 'cursor'
    49	 * @param {SessionFacts} [session] identity facts for generated hints; defaults to this process
    50	 * @param {string} [explicitEvent] the hook event name passed on the command line
    51	 *   (`--trigger`). Authoritative when present — the hooks manifest already keys
    52	 *   each command by event, so this needs no per-harness payload parsing. Cursor's
    53	 *   stop payload carries neither `hook_event_name` nor `event`, so without this
    54	 *   its checkpoint degraded to trigger 'unknown' and never satisfied the canary.
    55	 * @returns {any[]}
    56	 */
    57	export function normalizeHookPayload(raw, platform, session, explicitEvent) {
    58	  // Passthrough tier: the adapter formed the events; return them untouched.
    59	  if (raw && typeof raw === 'object' && raw.schema === 'baton/event@1') {
    60	    if (Array.isArray(raw.events)) return raw.events;
    61	    return [raw];
    62	  }
    63	
    64	  const { sessionHint, unstable } = deriveHint(raw, session);
    65	  /** @param {string} type @param {Record<string, any>} payload */
    66	  const event = (type, payload) => ({ type, payload, source: platform, sessionHint, unstable });
    67	  /** @param {string} trigger */
    68	  const note = (trigger) => event('note', { trigger, text: 'hook ' + trigger + ' observed on ' + platform });
    69	
    70	  const eventName =
    71	    (typeof explicitEvent === 'string' && explicitEvent.length > 0 && explicitEvent) ||
    72	    (raw && typeof raw === 'object'
    73	      ? (typeof raw.hook_event_name === 'string' && raw.hook_event_name) ||
    74	        (platform === 'cursor' && typeof raw.event === 'string' && raw.event) ||
    75	        null
    76	      : null);
    77	  if (!eventName) return [note('unknown')];
    78	
    79	  if (platform === 'claude-code') {
    80	    if (eventName === 'PostToolUse') {
    81	      const op = FILE_TOOL_OPS[raw.tool_name];
    82	      const path = raw.tool_input && typeof raw.tool_input === 'object' ? raw.tool_input.file_path : undefined;
    83	      if (op && typeof path === 'string') return [event('file.touch', { path, op })];
    84	      return [note('PostToolUse')];
    85	    }
    86	    if (eventName === 'StopFailure') {
    87	      const errorType = raw.error && typeof raw.error === 'object' && typeof raw.error.type === 'string' ? raw.error.type : 'unknown';
    88	      return [
    89	        event('note', {
    90	          trigger: 'StopFailure',
    91	          text: 'turn ended on API error: ' + errorType,
    92	          errorType,
    93	          structured: { kind: 'stop-failure', errorType },
    94	        }),
    95	      ];
    96	    }
    97	    return [note(eventName)];
    98	  }
    99	
   100	  // Codex and Cursor (and any future platform string): every lifecycle event
   101	  // is a note carrying its trigger; the harness is the source.
   102	  return [note(eventName)];
   103	}
